# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

#### Desktop: device online state never closed the loop — device up/down event names drifted, so the frontend missed the WS-driven events (no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **Symptom**: after a mobile device reconnects to the desktop WS through a saved connection (no pairing-code flow), the device list shows 0 online; after the mobile device disconnects, the desktop still shows it online
- **Root cause (event-name drift left behind by ticket 07)**: the WS-driven path (`devices_events.rs`, driven by `ws:client-connect/disconnect`) emits the SDK event names `device:connected` / `device:disconnected` (colon style, same as `task:status-changed`); the host `emit_event` passes event names through verbatim with no mapping. The frontend still subscribed to the pre-ticket-07 hyphenated names `device-connected` / `device-disconnected`, so **online/offline events never reached it**. The pairing/QR/reauth flow (`auth_http`) emitted the hyphenated name and kept the "authentication success = online" pseudo-online working, but the disconnect event (colon) was missed — online state lingered
- **Fix**: unify on the SDK colon constants — frontend `useDeviceCenter.ts` / `notifications.ts` subscribe to `device:connected` / `device:disconnected`; `auth_http`'s `emit_device_connected` now uses `EVENT_DEVICE_CONNECTED` with a canonical camelCase payload (same shape as `devices_events::event_payload`; frontend dedupes by fingerprint idempotently); `deviceOnlineKey` / display-name fallback accept both camelCase and snake_case payloads. The Rust structure lock (`event_names_match_frontend_keys`) now pins the frontend subscription files to the colon names verbatim so the drift cannot return
- **Verification**: terminal-session plugin vitest **44 + 73 related cases green** (new camelCase-payload and disconnect-loop cases; mutation checks: reverting the camelCase fallback or the colon subscription turns red) + Rust **449 passed**; desktop full vitest **1618/1619** (the only failure, `terminalPreview.test.ts`, is a pre-existing xterm + real-clock flake — proven with a stash comparison that HEAD fails identically). WASM artifact rebuilt with a fresh wasmHash. Not run: `cross-end-tests` (no protocol / ABI / WIT change; the event name is a desktop server-side internal subscription surface)

#### Desktop: the session-log detail pane collapsed to a single row — the height chain is unbroken across the page-transition container

- **Symptom (real machine, 1560×1080)**: opening any session left 「任务记录」 at roughly 28px — one row tall with a scrollbar stub — while ~740px below sat empty
- **Cause**: the height chain had a **non-flex hop** in it. `.ah-view` is a flex column of `height: 100%`, but the section-transition container `.page-swap` (a host-global class declaring only `position: relative`) sits between it and `.ah-lg`. `.ah-lg`'s `flex: 1` therefore targeted a block parent and was a **no-op** — it got no height to distribute, so `.ah-lg-detail` / `.ah-lg-events` `flex` fell through too. `.ah-lg`'s own comment still claimed the chain was `.ah-view` → `.ah-lg`, which stopped being true when the transition container was introduced
- **Fix (two scoped rules, `.page-swap`'s own `display` untouched)**: `.ah-view > .page-swap` takes the remaining height (`min-height: 0` is load-bearing — without it a flex item's automatic minimum size is its content and `flex: 1` cannot shrink), and `.ah-lg` anchors on `height: 100%` instead of the dead `flex: 1`. Turning `.page-swap` itself into a flex container would have made every tab root in all 6 sections a shrinkable flex item — a far wider blast radius than the two rules here
- **Primary-list scrolling is unchanged**: `.ah-lg` is content-sized there, overflow stays visible and propagates up to `.ah-view` (`overflow-y: auto`), whose reserved `scrollbar-gutter: stable` keeps the width pinned — no horizontal jitter reintroduced
- **Verification**: agent-hub vitest **531 passed / 0 failed** (new style guard `S11` in `styleGuards.test.ts`, 4 cases pinning the chain hop by hop — including an explicit assertion that `.ah-lg` has **no** `flex`, since restoring it is silent and re-breaks the pane). Mutation self-check killed 4/4 injected mutations (revert to `flex: 1`, drop the `.page-swap` rule, drop its `min-height: 0`, drop `.ah-lg-events`'s `min-height: 0`). `pnpm exec eslint` on the app: 0 errors. Confirmed by screenshot on the running app: detail pane now fills the viewport and scrolls internally, and the raw-JSONL tab renders as scannable one-row-per-record lines. Not run: wasm app packaging build, mobile, `cross-end-tests` (CSS-only change; no ABI / WIT / protocol / cross-end surface)

#### Desktop: codex session logs were parsed off the wrong face — the authoritative `item_completed` records now drive events and titles (no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **Root cause (2026-10-06 real-machine calibration, 14 rollouts / 994 lines)**: codex writes every conversation **twice** — an authoritative face (`event_msg.payload.type == "item_completed"`, `payload.item.type` ∈ `UserMessage` / `AgentMessage` / `CommandExecution` / `Reasoning`, **PascalCase**) and a fallback face (`response_item`) that repeats it verbatim **plus three synthetic injections per session** (developer `<skills_instructions>`, user `# AGENTS.md instructions for …`, user `<environment_context>`). The adapter only ever read the fallback face (its module doc admitted "never initialized codex on this machine"), so **all 14 session titles were `# AGENTS.md instructions for …`**, the real first prompt was pushed to the third bubble, and 96 reasoning summaries were lost (the fallback `reasoning` carries `encrypted_content` only)
- **Both faces now have a defined job**: `item_completed` is the authoritative face (session titles come from `UserMessage` only, never from an assistant greeting); the fallback face is buffered and settled at end of file — its `message` variant is dropped wholesale when the authoritative face is present, while `function_call(_output)` entries survive unless their `call_id` matches an authoritative `CommandExecution` (`CommandExecution.id === function_call.call_id` on real data). Tool calls the authoritative face never projects (`apply_patch` / `write_stdin`) therefore keep working instead of vanishing
- **Newly reachable**: `Reasoning.summary_text` (a **string array**, unlike the fallback's block array) renders as a system event; `CommandExecution` renders `exec · command · output` and flags a non-zero `exit_code` as an error. `token_usage_record` is explicitly **not** consumed — it is the same accounting as `token_count` (1:1 on real data), and taking both would double every session's tokens; verified unchanged (`sum(last_token_usage) == final total_token_usage`)
- **Raw JSONL tab is now usable**: every codex rollout's **first line** is a ~21KB `session_meta` carrying `base_instructions` (corpus mean 3.3KB/line, max 62KB), so the view opened onto a single unreadable blob. Lines beyond 1000 chars now collapse to a single ellipsized row with a per-line expand toggle (mirroring the existing chat-row collapse), short lines render in full, and an empty `raw` shows an explicit empty state instead of a blank card
- **Verification**: `agent-hub` crate `cargo test --lib` 218 passed / 0 failed (14 new codex cases across `codex/tests/{item_face_session,fallback_face_dedup}.rs`; inline cases moved into those files per the `pi/tests/` convention). Mutation self-check killed 11/11 injected mutations. Re-parsed all 14 real rollouts: titles are now the real prompts, no injection noise, reasoning present, token totals byte-identical. `agent-hub` vitest 527 passed / 0 failed (4 new raw-line cases; one of them caught a defect in the first implementation — short lines were picking up the collapsed class and being ellipsized at viewport width). `pnpm exec eslint` on the app: 0 errors. Not run: wasm app packaging build, mobile, `cross-end-tests` (no ABI / WIT / protocol / cross-end surface touched); one pre-existing red elsewhere — `wasm-apps/terminal-session/src/__tests__/terminalPreview.test.ts` `vi.waitFor` timeout — reproduces standalone and is unrelated to this change
- Also corrected `docs/commands.md`: only `terminal-session` has a `test:rust` script; the other desktop wasm apps run `cargo test` in `rust/`

#### Desktop: the first-party no-dialog directory list leaves the mechanism kernel — the host lib injects it at boot (ADR 0038 P0-2; no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **Product data moves to the host, the judgement stays in the kernel**: `FIRST_PARTY_TRUSTED_DIRS` (which plugin gets which directory without a dialog) is now the single list in `src-tauri/src/first_party_dirs.rs` — host lib, product truth — and reaches the mechanism through `PluginHost::new`'s new 6th argument → `WasmRuntime::new`/`with_config` → `FsAuthChecker::new/assemble`. The matching logic (`first_party_dir_matches_with_home`), the `TrustedDir` shapes and the read-model projection stay in `bedcode-wasm-core` (`security/fs_auth.rs`); an empty injected table means no exemption at all, so headless / test / non-desktop hosts keep byte-identical semantics
- **Why the list must not sit in the crate**: per ADR 0038 the kernel holds mechanism only, and this list is a product decision (which of our own apps are privileged, and why). Every entry in the new file names the code that consumes it, and `first_party_list_is_well_formed` pins the known consumers (`agent-hub` + `terminal-session`) so adding one forces an explicit justification
- **The auth-management read model now reads the injected table**: `plugin_auth_overview` takes the already-registered `FsAuthChecker` state and passes `first_party_trusted_dirs()` into `AuthPolicyStore::overview` (the global free function of the same name is gone). The wire shape (`FirstPartyDirEntry` = `pluginId` / `kind` / `value`) is unchanged, so the settings UI needs no change and its existing overview test still covers the visible-privileges contract
- **Verification**: `bedcode-wasm-core` `cargo test --lib` 660 passed / 1 failed (the only red is the pre-existing 5ms wall-clock flake `perf_p2_guest_ring_fetch_batch_curve`); `src-tauri` `cargo test --lib` 69 passed / 0 failed. Not run: `cross-end-tests` (no cross-end protocol surface touched by this entry), wasm app builds and `gen/android` gradlew (no plugin, WIT or Kotlin change), mobile (untouched)
- Two `src-tauri` integration targets (`ws_e2e`, `system_component_test`) are red in this workspace for an unrelated reason: their WASM fixtures (`bedcode_plugin_sdk_fixtures.ws.*.wasm` / `bedcode_plugin_system_test.wasm`) have no builder left after ticket 05b moved those tests to `src-tauri/tests/` — pre-existing, tracked separately

#### Desktop: mDNS self-advertisement moves out of the host —— `bedcode-discovery-engine` absorbs the advertiser (no ABI / WIT / protocol changes)

- **The whole host `src-tauri/src/mdns/` directory is removed**: `MdnsAdvertiser` (broadcasting the desktop's own `_bedcode._tcp.local.` for mobile discovery) plus `AdvertiseConfig` / `SERVICE_TYPE` move into `bedcode-discovery-engine` (`advertiser.rs` + `types.rs`, converted CRLF→LF), living in the same crate as the `host-mdns` capability domain — all mDNS mechanism now belongs to the capability crate, the host keeps zero mDNS code
- **The moved API keeps its shape, validation semantics and error texts**: the host's `crate::AppError` is replaced by the crate's own `AdvertiserError{InvalidInput, Internal}` (the dependency lock only allows discovery-engine → host-kit; the mechanism crate must not depend on host error types); the daemon model is consolidated in the next entry
- **All call sites now name the crate explicitly** (no forwarding shim left): `lib.rs` (construction site) / `system/app_context.rs` / `server/ports_impl.rs` / 4 integration tests in `src-tauri/tests/` / `cross-end-tests` (path dependency added)
- **Cleanup**: the `flume` dev-dependency is dropped from `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` (it served only the old mdns tests and moved to the crate's dev-dependencies); discovery-engine gains the `sync` tokio feature
- **Verification**: discovery-engine `cargo test --lib` 27 passed (18 existing + 9 advertiser cases); `cargo check --lib` and the 4 edited integration test binaries compile clean (lib unit tests 68/69 — the only red is the parallel session's in-flight pty-engine lock entry, unrelated). Later consolidated in the shared-daemon entry below (28 passed)

#### Desktop: the mDNS self-advertiser joins the shared daemon —— `MdnsAdvertiser` lands in the discovery-engine handle table (no ABI / WIT / protocol changes)

- **No more self-built `ServiceDaemon`**: the host advertiser's start/stop now route through `engine::advertise_inner` / `stop_advertise_inner` (owner=host), sharing one daemon with the peer-net node identity and plugin advertisements, none able to unregister another's service — the engine's “single global daemon creation point” claim becomes true (the dual-daemon 5353 contention is gone)
- **45s re-announce renewal added** (the engine attaches the renewal task): the old implementation registered once without renewing, so peers lost the broadcast after cache TTL — fixed as a side effect
- **Stop semantics unified with plugins**: removing the handle-table entry + cancelling renewal means stopped; unregister is best-effort (failure is only a warn, converging via cache TTL); the shared daemon is never shut down; the old “keep advertising on unregister failure for retry” branch is retired (its test becomes a state-machine defense against registration-layer failures)
- **Injection surface replaced by a port**: `DaemonFactory`/`FakeDaemon`/the `flume` dev-dep are gone; `AdvertiseTarget` carries the registration layer (production wraps engine + the global ports instance, tests record the invocation contract: owner=host / SERVICE_TYPE / escaped fullname); `is_advertising` is backed by the local handle id (owner=host entries vanish only via this surface's stop, so id ≡ table entry)
- **Assembly order verified**: `PluginHost::new` → `install_capability_domain_ports` → discovery PORTS install before the supervisor ever advertises, in production and in the cross-end rig alike (same constraint as peer-net); host call sites unchanged
- **Verification**: crate `cargo test --lib` 28 passed (18 engine + 10 advertiser); `cargo fmt --check` clean; src-tauri `cargo check --lib` passes

#### Desktop: the host-pty capability domain moves out of wasm-core whole — `bedcode-pty-engine` owns the WIT binding too (ADR 0039; no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **User ruling: no shims left, move everything that is not wasm-core mechanism, and if the WIT interface moves too, wire the host through a declared trait + static scan.** The `bedcode-host-kit` package already provides both halves (capability module self-report via `inventory` + whitelist verification in `module.rs`/`registry.rs`; host port dispatch via `HostPorts::domain_ports` in `ports.rs`), so PTY becomes the **fifth domain following the exact http / ws / peer-net / mdns template** — nothing new was invented
- **`bedcode-pty-engine` is promoted from engine crate to capability-domain crate**: it now carries the `bindgen!` provider-side generation, the `HostModule` self-report, the 6 `host-pty` primitives, and the whole domain mechanism (handle registry, per-plugin quota, `pty:exit` assembly, rate-limited `pty:output` notify sink). It depends on `bedcode-host-kit` + `wasmtime` + `bedcode-server-base` and **not** on wasm-core. This **reverses ADR 0038 D1's "zero wasm dependency"** for this crate (the engine modules stay business-free and reusable; the crate granularity now pulls in wasmtime, same trade-off as the four existing domains)
- **Boundary = `PtyPorts`, a narrow port trait** (consumer declares, host implements, following `HttpPorts`). Exactly five things stay in the host as AGENTS §5.1.3 thin shells: `check_permission` (security gate), `publish` (bus delivery surface), `config` (`AppConfig` snapshot), `block_on_any` (the single sync↔async bridge — ambient runtime + actix `current_thread` re-entrancy workarounds are measured artifacts and must not be duplicated), `spawn_task` (ambient-runtime task spawn). Registration is two-level: process-level for host lifecycle actions, instance-level so guest calls hit **that instance's** `PermissionManager`
- **wasm-core keeps only the adapter** `host_api/pty.rs` (`HostPtyPorts` + `install` + `HOST_MODULE_NAME`); `host_api/pty_output.rs`, `src/pty.rs` (the pure `pub use` shim), `src/enums/pty_status.rs`, and the host `lib.rs` `pty` re-export are **deleted** — every call site now names `bedcode_pty_engine::plugin_binding::*` explicitly, and `component.rs` drops its own `impl host_pty::Host` + `add_to_linker` row (same-name/different-type traits on both sides would otherwise register the interface twice)
- **WSL distro listing stays in wasm-core** (`system/wsl.rs`): it is a `host-platform` platform fact, orthogonal to PTY (ADR 0038 E3 ①) — only 4 references in `host_api/platform.rs` changed
- **Lock polarity flipped** (D6): `pty_shim_file_contains_no_definitions` retired with the shim, replaced by `pty_module_must_not_return_to_wasm_core` (the kernel must not regain a PTY module/file/shim name) plus a reverse assertion in `wasm_core_whole_crate_lock.rs`. The lock compares whole trimmed lines rather than substrings — the lock's own doc comment and forbidden-name list contain the forbidden shapes, so substring matching makes the lock flag itself (a lock that flags itself is a lock that spins)
- **Tests re-homed by "who owns the truth"**: 39 domain-behavior cases (real PTY output, quota/ring-size arbitration, exit-event shape, notify coalescing, and the ring-internal views `watermarks()` / `chunk_count()` that only an in-crate test can read) moved into pty-engine with a mini-host fixture whose five port methods map one-to-one onto the real adapter; **real bus delivery stays host-side** (`pty_e2e` with a real `MessageBus` + real component instantiation, and `src-tauri/tests/pty_session_chain.rs` end-to-end); source-text wiring drift locks stay in wasm-core (`host_api/tests/pty_wiring.rs`). Fixture owner ids were renamed `com.bedcode.*` → `pty-test.*` so the capability-crate product-id lock keeps scanning this file instead of the file being hidden
- **Verification**: `bedcode-pty-engine` `cargo test --lib` 94 passed / 0 failed (three consecutive runs); `bedcode-wasm-core` pty-scoped tests 33 passed / 0 failed (incl. 5 real-component `pty_e2e` cases); host locks `wasm_core_whole_crate_lock` 3/3, `capability_crates_no_product_ids` 6/6, `capability_crates_unit_tests_only` 5/5; `src-tauri` `cargo check --lib` clean; `pty_session_chain_flow` end-to-end passed
- Not run / not mine: `cross-end-tests` (no cross-end protocol / auth / terminal surface changed), the wasm app builds and `gen/android` gradlew (no plugin, WIT or Kotlin change), mobile (untouched); the 5 `security::fs_auth` failures seen in one full wasm-core run come from a **parallel in-flight session** editing `security/*` (mtimes 13:52-14:03 during this work) and are outside this change's scope

#### Desktop: engine faces and host thin-shells leave the mechanism kernel — `bedcode-pty-engine` is born and auth/session bridges move back to the host (ADR 0038; no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **`bedcode-wasm-core` now holds wasm mechanism only** (user ruling: the crate should be single-purpose and reusable). Three kinds of non-mechanism code left: (A) the PTY engine (2,469 lines of portable_pty wrapping, zero wasm dependencies) moved out into a new engine crate `bedcode-desktop/packages/bedcode-pty-engine/` — downstream-only dependencies (`bedcode-server-base` + third-party), directly path-dependable by any Tauri host or any terminal-needing program; the host-pty WIT binding face stays in wasm-core (`host_api/{pty,pty_output}.rs`, it shares the `component.rs` impl-Host assembly) and `src/pty.rs` became a pure `pub use` shim (type identity is unique — one `PtySessionStatus`, no split definitions at `PtyTerminated::status`). The engine's two `AppConfig::global()` reads became constructor injection via `PtyEngineConfig` (defaults 16/4096, same as before). (B/C) the auth-center bridge (`enforce_connection_policy` fail-closed verdict + `session_active` gate) and the session narrow-forwarding layer (`session_gateway.rs`) moved **back to the host lib** — they are host thin-shells (ADR 0022 categories ② and ④); the wasm-core registry / WIT faces (`host_api/{auth,auth_center}.rs`) and `invoke_auth_method` (merged into `auth_center.rs`) stay as mechanism. `test_tokens.rs` stays in wasm-core always-compiled (it depends on crate-internal `test_seed_plugin_secret`; integration tests cannot see a dependency's cfg(test) items)
- **P0-1 closed with ticket 05**: `session_gateway` had drifted from zero-parsing narrow forwarding into product wire it does not own (8 hardcoded `com.bedcode.terminal-session session-*` api names, named product params, a `running`-filter question to the product) — B1/B4/B6 inside the mechanism crate. Moving it back to the host returns the product api names and parameter shapes to the host and the kernel is product-wire-free again
- **Test asset rework (user ruling: wasm-core holds no cross-crate integration tests at all)**: every wasm-core test that loads real product plugin artifacts moved to `src-tauri/tests/` as standalone binaries — `session_e2e` (3,720 lines), `ws_e2e` (1,953), `ws_output_perf`, `auth_center_perf`, `system_component_test`; `task_e2e` (ai-chatbox real-artifact cases) and `a03_probe` (product-closure + production async-store cases) split out of their files. Mechanism-only tests on SDK fixtures (`plugin-sdk-fixtures`) stay. The scaffold they needed moved up as always-compiled public test support (`src/test_support.rs`: `setup_wasm_runtime` / `plugin_db_root` / `session_plugin_db_guard` / `registry_gate` / `hold_registry_desk` / `reset` / `lock_auth_center_desk`; plus `host_api::ws` pub, `ws::purge_for_plugin`, `LoadedWasmPlugin::call_capability_export`, `host_api::grant_permissions` promoted to always-compiled pub), which doubles as the executable example of how a third-party host assembles this crate
- **Locks and registry updated together (ticket 06)**: `SPLIT_CRATES` (single source at `bedcode-wasm-core/src/crate_boundary_lock.rs`) registers `bedcode-pty-engine`; lib `crate_boundary_lock` gains the `pty-engine → base` and `wasm-core → pty-engine` edges in both `ALLOWED_DOWNWARD_EDGES` and `REQUIRED_DOWNWARD_EDGES`; lib `Cargo.toml` declares the crate explicitly (assertion ③: the host manifest names every split product — the host does not consume it directly, it arrives via the wasm-core shim). Anti-duplicate shim locks extended: `enums.rs` wire-shim lock now also scans `enums/pty_status.rs` (shimmed, truth moved to pty-engine) and lib.rs gained `pty_shim_file_contains_no_definitions` pinning `src/pty.rs` to re-export only
- **Verification**: wasm-core `cargo test --lib` 709 passed / 1 failed (the only failure is the pre-existing 5ms wall-clock flake `perf_p2_guest_ring_fetch_batch_curve`; 05c baseline was 708/1, +1 is this ticket's new shim lock); `cargo check` clean. Full crate+host suites and `cross-end-tests` are ticket 07 (independent acceptance)
- Not run: `cross-end-tests` (no cross-end protocol / auth / terminal surface changed), the wasm app builds and `gen/android` gradlew (no plugin, WIT or Kotlin change), and mobile (untouched)

#### Build infra: the sccache premise was wrong, and the target-size reporter was blind to the largest bucket and dangerous about one live directory

- **sccache does not cache what this repo assumed, and the docs said otherwise.** Root `.cargo/config.toml` and `docs/knowledge/build-process.md` both asserted that cargo enables incremental only for local crates, that sccache passes `-Zincremental` through, and that "full builds of dependency crates are cached as usual" — with a 2026-10-05 measurement attached (host-kit: wasmtime full build 3m34s → 37s after clean, 244 compiles 100% hit). **That measurement does not reproduce on the current toolchain (sccache 0.18 + Rust 1.98)**, and the mechanism behind it was never true: cargo's dev profile passes `-C incremental` for *every* crate, and sccache 0.18 **silently skips** those calls. The symptom is a distinctive "empty request" in the counters: `Compile requests` +1 while `executed` / `hits` / `misses` all stay put
- **Measured coverage: ~21%, missing exactly the expensive part.** In the `server-libs` bucket, 1049 ordinary crates — including wasmtime, tauri and actix — are all skipped, while only the 289 non-incremental units (111 proc-macro `.so` + 178 build scripts) reach the cache. A/B proof, reproducible in ~1 minute: `sccache --zero-stats` → `touch src/lib.rs` → `cargo build` reports `4 requests / 0 executed`; the same action under `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` reports `3 requests / 2 executed / 2 misses` and writes cache entries
- **The tradeoff is left open rather than silently flipped.** Actually getting the documented benefit needs `CARGO_INCREMENTAL = { value = "0", force = false }` in `[env]`; the price is losing cargo's own incremental compilation (any edit recompiles the whole crate), which is a global performance decision affecting both ends and CI — so it is **not** enabled unilaterally. Both docs now state the measured behaviour, the reproduction steps, and the cost of the fix; the ticket-F row in `build-process.md` moves from "rejected → reintroduced" to "rejected → reintroduced → partly overturned", noting that the original 2026-09-26 rejection reason still holds
- **Diagnostic hint added so the next person does not repeat the misdiagnosis**: when sccache looks broken, check whether `executed` is 0 — that means everything is being skipped, not that nothing is being hit. (An earlier pass in this session misread the same counters and concluded the wrapper was a dead pass-through; it is not.)
- **`check-target-size.js` never reported `target/host-kits`** — the largest bucket in the repo (14.5G here, 19.7G before this round's cleanup) landed there after wasm-core-lib-split tickets 03/05 and wasm-core-whole-crate ticket 02, but `rootTargetDirs` only listed `target/server-libs`, so `pnpm run target:size` silently omitted it. It is now registered
- **And its "safe to delete" verdict was both wrong and aimed at a live directory.** `legacyTargetParents: ['packages', 'wasm-apps']` labels any `packages/<crate>/target` it finds as "可安全删除"; separately, `packages/target` was not detected at all, because the loop only probes `<parent>/<crate>/target` and never the parent-level landing spot. `packages/target/fixtures` (533.82 MB) is in fact **referenced by literal path** from `bedcode-wasm-core/src/test_support.rs` (the fixture-artifact path `../target/fixtures`, itself a residue of two `.cargo/config.toml` files each writing one `..` too many). Following the report's advice would have destroyed in-flight work. The script now probes the parent-level spot too and consults a new `legacyTargetLive` map: a registered directory is reported as `遗留·在用 ⚠ 不可删` with the referencing code named, and is excluded from the reclaimable total (a separate `⛔` line reports how much was held back)
- **The mobile twin was deliberately not touched**: `bedcode-mobile/scripts/check-target-size.js` has the same parent-level blind spot, but the mobile end currently has no target directories at all, so fixing it would be a speculative change. Noted rather than done
- **Verification**: `node --check` clean and `pnpm run target:size` re-run on both sides of the fix — it now lists `target/host-kits` first, and `packages/target` as `遗留·在用` with its 533.82 MB held out of the reclaimable figure; `.cargo/config.toml` re-parsed (valid, `rustc-wrapper` intact, no `CARGO_INCREMENTAL` added) and `cargo check` still succeeds through the wrapper
- Not run: `cross-end-tests`, the wasm app builds and `gen/android` gradlew, and mobile (no product, WIT or Kotlin change; this round is build tooling and documentation only)

#### Desktop: the split crates now carry unit tests only — two integration test targets moved to the host, plus a purity lock that cannot be bypassed quietly (no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **New lock, `src-tauri/tests/capability_crates_unit_tests_only.rs` (5 cases)**: a crate-root `tests/` directory is a separate test binary that can only reach the crate through its `pub` API, i.e. an extra *outward behaviour surface* on a crate that is supposed to be a reusable engine. It also has a dependency-graph cost: `tests/` can only add dependencies through `[dev-dependencies]`, and a dev edge is a real edge in the graph. The lock pins two criteria — (1) governed crates have no crate-root `tests/` / `benches/` / `examples/` directory and no `[[test]]` / `[[bench]]` / `[[example]]` section (all aliases of the same shape); (2) their `[dev-dependencies]` contain no `bedcode*` crate. External dev dependencies (`tempfile`, `tracing-subscriber`, extra `tokio` features) are deliberately out of scope: those serve unit-test fixtures inside `src/`, not a cross-crate composition
- **Why the existing locks were not enough**: each transport face's `dependency_direction_lock` parses `[dependencies]` **only** (and explicitly asserts dev-dependencies do not pollute its verdict), so dev edges have zero coverage; `crate_boundary_lock` asks whether an edge is legal, not whether the crate grew a `tests/` directory; and `capability_crates_no_product_ids.rs` excludes `tests`-path files from its scan by design — which is exactly the shape this lock forbids. The three are complementary and none was removed
- **Coverage is derived from the directory convention, not a hand-written list**: governed surface = every `bedcode-*` directory under `bedcode-desktop/packages/`, so a new crate is in scope the moment it lands (same technique as `empty_dir_lock.rs`; a hand-written list is a zero-cost back door — delete one line, lose a slice of coverage, lock stays green). The single exemption bucket `PENDING_GOVERNANCE` holds only `bedcode-wasm-core` (the kernel is being reworked by another in-flight stream; the user ruled it out of scope this round), it demands a non-empty reason string, and it fails if an entry no longer exists on disk — so it cannot rot into a garbage drawer
- **C-5 pins the migrated files from the front, so "delete the offending test" is not a free move**: C-2 / C-3 forbid integration tests living in a crate but cannot stop someone from deleting one outright — the lock would stay green while coverage silently evaporated. So the lock also asserts both files exist host-side with their case counts (`tests/link_crypto_http.rs` ≥ 4, `tests/error_envelope_ipc.rs` ≥ 3)
- **Both integration test targets migrated to the host, zero new dependencies there**: `packages/bedcode-server-http/tests/link_crypto_http.rs` → `src-tauri/tests/link_crypto_http.rs` (its subject is a **three-crate composition**: `TrafficFilter` × `link_crypto` / `TrafficFilterChain` × `bedcode-crypto-engine`) and `packages/bedcode-server-base/tests/error_envelope_ipc.rs` → `src-tauri/tests/error_envelope_ipc.rs` (`AppError` × the Tauri IPC serialisation layer). Both now reach their types directly (`use bedcode_server_base::error::AppError`) instead of routing through a `pub use` re-export, and the http crate's test-only `bedcode-crypto-engine` + `base64` dev-dependencies are gone — `cargo tree -e dev --depth 1` for that crate now shows no bedcode crate at all. Ticket 08's original ruling ("http face, so zero new horizontal edges") had narrowed the choice to *which of the two tested crates* should host the test and so paid in a `tests/` directory plus a test-only internal edge; the host was the third option and the one where every dependency is a production dependency
- **The mutation self-check found two real fail-open holes in the new lock's own manifest parser** (both are the lock silently losing sight of a dependency, i.e. green instead of red): (1) the **dotted table form** `[dev-dependencies.bedcode-crypto-engine]` — cargo supports it fully, the parser only looked for entry names *inside* a `[dev-dependencies]` section, so an injected dev edge passed C-3 unnoticed; (2) the `[target.'cfg(unix)'.dependencies]` guard was gated on "already inside the requested section", making it unreachable whenever the header appeared before that section — the C-1 fixture killed it. Both are fixed, `section_header` now normalises all three TOML header shapes, and C-1 carries a positive fixture for the dotted form (attributed to the correct section, both directions). Also fixed: a dependency declaration wrapped across lines was being collected as a second, valid-looking entry name, so the parser now requires braces to balance on an entry line
- **Mutation self-check, all four die**: a crate-root `tests/` dir → C-2; a `[[test]]` section → C-2; a dev-only internal crate dependency (dotted form) → C-3; lowering the pinned case count of a migrated file → C-5. Every mutation was reverted with a precise replacement (the pty-engine manifest is untracked and belongs to the other stream, so `git checkout` was not an option)
- **Verification**: new lock 5 passed; `src-tauri/tests/link_crypto_http.rs` 4 passed and `src-tauri/tests/error_envelope_ipc.rs` 3 passed at their new host-side location; `bedcode-server-http` 81 passed / 0 failed, `bedcode-server-base` 24 passed / 0 failed (+1 ignored doc-test), both after the dev-dependency removal; rustfmt clean on the touched files
- **Explicitly out of scope, and deliberately so**: `bedcode-host-kit` lives at the repo-root `packages/` (not `bedcode-desktop/packages/`) and its `tests/forced_link.rs` + `tests/forced_link_absent.rs` pair is a cross-crate integration test **by design** — two test binaries pinning both sides of "a missing forced-reference line loses the capability registration" against a probe fixture crate, so moving it means moving the fixture too (separate ticket). `peer-net`'s 5 targets plus its example are the same story. Both are recorded in the new lock's header so the omission reads as a decision rather than an oversight
- Not run: `cross-end-tests` (no cross-end protocol / auth / terminal surface changed), the wasm app builds and `gen/android` gradlew (no plugin, WIT or Kotlin change), and mobile (untouched)

#### Desktop: capability crates get a semantic re-entry lock, `tauri` stops reaching the server crates through the base layer, and the legacy wire-shape DTOs stop compiling into production (no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **New lock, `src-tauri/tests/capability_crates_no_product_ids.rs` (6 cases)**: every boundary lock in the repo is a *structural* lock — `crate_boundary_lock` asks whether an edge is legal, the transport crates' `dependency_direction_lock` asks whether a horizontal edge exists, `HostModuleDesc`'s product-noun token lock asks whether a descriptor is clean. **None of them asks whether the code sitting on a legal edge is business code**, so a `com.bedcode.terminal-session` literal in a capability crate passed everything green. This lock scans the production text of the 8 capability / transport crates under `bedcode-desktop/packages/` (the 7 already registered plus `bedcode-pty-engine`, which entered scope when its extraction from the kernel landed). Comments are stripped (quote-aware — a `//` inside a string literal is not a comment, and a naive strip would silently drop real hits), `#[cfg(test)]` regions and `tests`-path files are excluded (golden-shape fixtures reproduce product bytes by design), and two criteria fire: unregistered product plugin ids, and AGENTS §5.3 retired host surfaces (including the two dead constants deleted in this round, so "deleted" becomes "deleted and locked")
- **The lock cannot be quietly weakened, by four independent proofs**: `scanner_is_not_vacuous` (the scanner must find real hits and must not fake it by dropping everything), `placeholder_segments_do_not_mask_real_product_segments` (the placeholder exemption is a closed list of neutral words; each real product name is listed as a counter-example), `every_registered_crate_is_present_and_scan_coverage_is_complete` (every `bedcode-*` directory under `packages/` must sit in either the scanned bucket or the pending bucket — deleting a registry entry, which is otherwise a zero-cost back door, turns the lock red), `registered_product_id_exceptions_are_pinned_by_content` (the single registered exception is pinned **by literal content**, not by file, so adding/removing/re-pointing one takeover row turns it red). Four mutations were injected and all four died: a product id in capability-crate production code → C-4; a retired-surface token → C-6; removing a crate from the registry → C-3 plus the exception cross-check; writing a deleted constant back verbatim → C-6
- **One registered exception, with its classification written down**: `bedcode-server-http`'s `LEGACY_HTTP_PLUGIN_ALIASES` (retired plugin id → takeover id) is AGENTS §5.1.3 ③ "generic registry and addressing" — it is id-to-id addressing, it describes no session / terminal / transfer semantics, and it does not decide anything about a plugin's business; the takeover plugin registers its own routes and answers for itself. Plugin identity is a kernel-side concern, so the takeover relation between retired ids belongs to the kernel. An earlier pass of this review judged it a B1/B5 violation; that judgement was too heavy and is corrected here
- **`bedcode-server-base`: `tauri` is now an optional feature (`tauri-compat`, default on)**: the crate is the leaf foundation of every server face, and a GUI framework in the foundation means no non-Tauri host (headless service / CLI / embedded) can pull it — even though all they want is an error type, constants and port traits. The only use is one `impl From<tauri::Error> for AppError`, and the orphan rule forces that impl to live in the crate that defines `AppError`, so the price is explicit and documented in the manifest: with the feature off, callers must `map_err` at the boundary instead of using `?`. `cargo tree --no-default-features` now shows zero tauri crates (one with defaults); every desktop consumer keeps the default and behaviour is unchanged
- **Legacy wire-shape DTOs stop compiling into production — the gate is the type system**: `dtos.rs` gates the session / file / git DTO groups with `#[cfg(test)]`, so production code referencing them fails to **compile** rather than failing a lock. Enabling the gate required deleting a leftover `pub use … file_dto::{…}` in `config_dto.rs` (zero consumers repo-wide, plus a bad-cohesion edge where the config domain re-exported file-browsing types). `config_dto` is the one group that stays ungated: `bedcode-wasm-core`'s `session_e2e` compares the plugin's HTTP output against it as a cross-crate golden, and a dependency is compiled without `cfg(test)` — a known directional coupling (mechanism kernel → transport crate's product DTO), noted at both ends, closable once that side owns its golden shape
- **Two dead constants deleted, one comment de-productised**: `PLUGIN_SESSION_RING_FETCH_MAX_BYTES` and `ENV_BEDCODE_SESSION_ID` had zero consumers (the same-named consts in `wasm-apps/` are a plugin's own). `PLUGIN_HTTP_MAX_ENDPOINTS_PER_PLUGIN = 64` keeps its value but loses the "calibrated against one plugin's 41 endpoints" wording, which documented a product shape inside a kernel quota
- **Host build repair (not part of the review, but it blocked verification)**: the kernel-purity stream left `src-tauri/src/utils/auth.rs` re-exporting `bedcode_wasm_core::utils::auth::test_tokens`, a path that no longer exists, so the lib's **test** build failed with E0432. The re-export has no consumer in the host (integration tests cannot see a dependency's `cfg(test)` items), so it was removed and the module doc now states why the fixture stays kernel-side
- **Verification**: host `cargo test --no-fail-fast` 105 passed / 0 failed (lib 76 + 12 integration targets + doc-tests) — including `pty_session_chain` and `wasm_bridge_bench`, which load real wasip3 fixture components (the fixture compile hit the cache this round, so the load step reported 0.0s); `bedcode-server-http` 81 + 4 green, `bedcode-server-base` 24 + 3 green; `bedcode-server-base --no-default-features` compiles with zero tauri crates in the tree; rustfmt clean on every file this round touched; clippy adds no new findings in the touched files (the two warnings in `bedcode-server-base` are pre-existing, in `src/ports.rs` and `tests/error_envelope_ipc.rs`)
- Not run: `cross-end-tests` (no cross-end protocol / auth / terminal surface changed), the wasm app builds and `gen/android` gradlew (no plugin, WIT or Kotlin change), and mobile (untouched)

#### Desktop: the whole plugin-mechanism kernel moves out of the host binary into a reusable crate `bedcode-wasm-core` (ADR 0037; no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **What changed**: `src-tauri/src/wasm_core/` (54,394 lines / 119 files) plus the engine faces it lives with — `db/` (schema.sql single source of truth), `pty/`, `enums/`, `system/{config,opener,process}`, `utils/auth/auth_center.rs`, `utils/session_gateway.rs`, `utils/auth/test_tokens.rs` and the `HostBusPort` — moved as a whole into a new reusable crate at `bedcode-desktop/packages/bedcode-wasm-core/` (spec M1–M11, tickets 02–04). The host keeps only composition roots plus `pub use` shims in `lib.rs` / `system.rs` / `utils.rs` / `utils/auth.rs` / `server/ports_impl.rs`, so every existing `crate::wasm_core::*` / `crate::db::*` / `crate::pty::*` / `crate::enums::*` reference (lib, the 5 integration tests, cross-end-tests) compiles unchanged (D3)
- **Why**: the mechanism kernel was 54k lines growing inside the bin crate — not a library, not independently compilable / testable / publishable — and the mechanism-vs-host boundary had never been drawn (`db` truth in lib, mechanism in the bin, engines scattered). ADR 0036's "mechanism and its truth on the same side" had exactly two answers until now. Measured cost of extracting: only ~4,500 lines of lib modules needed to move or become ports (5 dependency types were already packages/ crates or shims)
- **The boundary, drawn on the way out**: only **one** port (`PeerCtxProvider`, injected via `PluginHost::new`'s new 5th parameter — lib passes `Some(peer_net_cmds::peer_ctx)`, headless test rigs pass `None` and keep the exact `HEADLESS_UNAVAILABLE` semantics). No DbPort / PtyPort / AppHandlePort / ConfigPort: `app_handle` was already injected and stored in `WasmHostContext`, `db` / `pty` moved with the kernel, and the mdns adapter's global lookup now goes through a crate-internal host-context registry (OnceLock<Weak<WasmHostContext>>) installed at the single-assembly-point `install_capability_domain_ports` (D10, same single-entry discipline as the 2026-10-05 port-assembly lesson)
- **WIT / ABI untouched**: `world plugin`'s 22 imports unchanged, `ABI_VERSION` unchanged — already-installed `.wasm` files need no rebuild. Mobile untouched (ADR 0018 contract independence)
- **Verification**: crate `cargo check --lib` green; crate `cargo test --lib` 784 passed / 2 failed (both pre-existing baselines: `session_e2e::test_session_task_domain_closed_loop` red since 9-30, and the 5ms wall-clock flake `perf_p2_guest_ring_fetch_batch_curve`); src-tauri `cargo check` / `cargo check --tests` green, lib `cargo test --lib` 76 passed / 0 failed; cross-end-tests `cargo check --tests` green (`PluginHost::new` 5th param synced). Lock consolidation (ticket 05): `SPLIT_CRATES` registry moved up to the crate as single source (lib → crate one-way reference, `bedcode-wasm-core` itself registered), `hot_path_logging_lock` `LOCKED_SITES` paths repointed into the crate, and a new structural lock `src-tauri/tests/wasm_core_whole_crate_lock.rs` asserts the host side holds no `wasm_core` implementation files and the shims are pure `pub use`
- Not run: wasm app full builds (no wasm-app change), `gen/android` gradlew (no Kotlin change), mobile (untouched)

#### Desktop: the SQLite capability domain crate is withdrawn — the plugin-facing database mechanism (13 primitives) stays inside the wasm core (ADR 0036; no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **What changed**: `bedcode-desktop/packages/bedcode-sqlite-engine/` no longer exists. The `host-database` (5) / `host-plugin-database` (5) / `host-storage` (3) primitives are back in `wasm_core/host_api/{database,storage}.rs` with their `impl … Host for WasmPluginState` blocks back in `manager/runtime/component.rs` (and back in that file's local `add_to_linker` table); the SQLite engine face (`src-tauri/src/db.rs` + `db/{database,models,operations}.rs` + `db/schema.sql`) is back at `src-tauri/src/db/`. All 13 primitive names, permission bits, error strings, structured log fields, the table-name depth defence, the timeout / row / byte guards, the batch all-or-nothing semantics, the system-space fail-closed guard and the kv owner partitioning are byte-for-byte the same — only the location moved back
- **Why**: one question had two answers. The truth these primitives guard is already the host's: `schema.sql` creates `plugin_auth_policies` / `plugin_auth_records` / `plugin_secrets` / `plugin_storage`, and 13 host files (`security/{auth_policy,approval,strategy,network_auth,fs_auth}`, `wasm_core/storage.rs`, `host_api/{auth,context}`, `manager/{validation,host/api_bridge}`, …) read and write those tables through `Database`; the authority for authorisation is the host's `PermissionManager` plus `host_api::check_permission`. With the mechanism face in another crate, "where a plugin's authorisation records and plugin data live, and who may read or write them" was half in each place. ADR 0022's B3 requires the mechanism and its truth to sit on the same side — unlike the four capability domains that stayed in crates (mdns / websocket / peer / http), whose truth is anonymous engine resources the host never reads
- **Measured cost removed**: the capability crate depended unconditionally on `wasmtime` (component-model) + `wit-bindgen` + `bedcode-host-kit` + `bedcode-plugin-api` + `inventory`, with no feature gate on `plugin_binding`, so every consumer that only wanted `Database` also compiled the component-model stack — 387 crates in the graph versus 293 for the engine face alone (the ~94 extra were all `wasmtime* / cranelift* / wasm-tools / wit-bindgen* / wasmtime-wasi`). It also bound an ABI edit to an engine rebuild, and the provider-side `bindgen!` produced a `Host` trait that is the same name but a different type than the host's guest-side one, forcing both impl sets to move together to avoid `defined twice`
- **The port seam stays, demoted to a test seam**: `host_api/sqlite_ports.rs` keeps the `SqlitePorts` trait (permission gate / library handles / the single sync↔async bridge / kv store and capability routing) and `host_api/sqlite.rs` keeps its host implementation, because that is what lets the domain logic run against a fake port (`host_api/sqlite_scaffold.rs`) without constructing a full `WasmHostContext` — 40 existing cases (statement timeout, row and byte caps, authorizer depth defence, batch atomicity, kv isolation, system-space guard) came back with the implementation rather than being rewritten. What disappeared with the crate: the process-wide `OnceLock` singleton, the instance-level `domain_ports` registration, `inventory` self-report, the forced-reference line and the `sqlite` whitelist entry. The port is now taken per call from the calling context (`sqlite::ports_for(&ctx)`, borrowed, zero allocation; `plugin_db`'s lazy-creation future borrows the context, the same shape as the existing `DbScope::get_or_create_plugin_db`)
- **Locks shrank but still bite**: the crate-boundary lock drops its four `bedcode-sqlite-engine` entries, the module whitelist drops `sqlite`, and the routable-capability closed-table lock now points `host-storage` at `src/wasm_core/host_api/sqlite_ports.rs` instead of the deleted crate path. No lock silently stopped checking — the whitelist's missing direction still catches a capability crate that failed to link, and `test_loaded_plugin_component_roundtrip` still fails loudly at instantiation if an import goes unregistered
- **Verification**: desktop `cargo check --lib --tests` and `cargo clippy --lib --tests` clean (the `dead_code` notes on `database.rs` / `storage.rs` are inherent to this shape — the only production callers are `bindgen!`-generated trait impls the linker calls at runtime, same as before the withdrawal); `cargo test --lib` green for `host_api::database` (32), `host_api::storage` (4), `db::` (4), `component::tests` (20, including the roundtrip), `crate_boundary` (8), `capability::tests` (6) and `empty_dir_lock` (4); full `--lib` run leaves only the pre-existing `session_e2e::test_session_task_domain_closed_loop` failure already recorded for tickets 06/07/08
- Not run: `cross-end-tests` (no cross-end protocol change), the wasm app builds and `gen/android` Kotlin (no plugin or Kotlin change), and mobile (untouched)

#### Desktop: the mechanism kernel plus the mdns / websocket / peer / http capability domains are out of the kernel, the assembly is automatic and locked, and the runtime-routing table reached its first migrated domain (no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **Scope note**: tickets 07/08 of this work (the SQLite capability domain crate) were implemented here and then **withdrawn the same day by ADR 0036** — see the entry above. What survives from that pair is nothing: `bedcode-sqlite-engine` is gone and the 13 primitives plus the SQLite engine face are back in `wasm_core` / `src/db/`. The rest of this entry stands for the mechanism kernel plus the mdns / websocket / peer / http domains
- **Why it had to move at all**: the position of a capability implementation was historical accident, not a boundary. The host assembled 22 interfaces one hardcoded `add_to_linker` line each, so "move the implementation out of the kernel" and "wire it up" were the same edit — which is why the implementations could not leave. Now a capability crate submits itself through the mechanism kernel's module registry (`packages/bedcode-host-kit`, the dual-end anchor): `add_to_linker` keeps one table for the interfaces that stay in the core and one loop for everything self-reported. Adding a capability domain is a dependency plus one whitelist entry — no host wiring edit
- **Automatic must not mean silently drifting**: a whitelist lock compares the collected set against a tree-side constant in **both** directions (an unreviewed capability is red; a capability whose crate failed to link is red), the forced-reference lines sit next to that constant, and a missing module fails **loudly at instantiation** naming the module plus the two different remedies. The premise that made this subtle is now automated evidence, not a comment: `inventory` submissions are linker-section statics, so an unreferenced rlib contributes nothing. Two test binaries pin it — one references a probe crate (the collection is exactly that probe), one does not (the collection is empty)
- **Runtime routing reached its first migrated domain, and the closed table is now locked**: `host-mdns` joins `host-storage` in the routable-capability table, with its five forwarding methods across all three layers (capability-domain port / host forwarding function / provider narrow port). The table carries the method prefix per capability, and a lock compares the three layers **item by item** — the silent failure this prevents is "the domain port declares forwarding, the host has no matching function", where the registry considers the capability routable, every call returns `None`, and the capability quietly reverts to the host primitive with no error anywhere. The probe table is now derived (routable ∪ probe-only) instead of listing the routable entry twice
- **The one thing this deliberately does not open**: forwarding never carried the caller's `plugin_id` — it was only used to detect self-calls — while every capability domain partitions its truth by caller `plugin_id`. A system component taking over a capability therefore lands in its own namespace, owns the caller's handles, and receives the discovery events the caller subscribed to. That is true of **every** capability domain, so the ticket wired the mechanism without opening the entry: `world plugin-system` gained no `export host-mdns`, no component can provide those five functions, and routing is unreachable by construction. Evidence, three candidate shapes and the "do not casually change the WIT" constraint are filed as ticket 10
- **Crate boundary lock now covers nine crates** (the six server splits + the mechanism kernel + the two capability domains) with a path column, because the kernel lives at the repository root and a name-only table would have reported it as "split product missing" — a lock that silently does not check is worse than no lock. It found a real edge on the first pass: all three transport crates depend on the mechanism kernel (each holds a `plugin_binding`), now registered as an allowed downward edge and required in production dependencies
- **Verification**: desktop `cargo test --no-fail-fast`: `--lib` 820 passed / 1 failed / 1 ignored (the failure is the pre-existing stale `session_e2e::test_session_task_domain_closed_loop` assertion recorded in tickets 06/07/08 — this ticket touches neither; accounting versus ticket 08: 816 + 4 new = 820 ✅), all nine integration targets green; `bedcode-host-kit` 16 green; `bedcode-discovery-engine` 18 green (14 + 4 routing: forwarded calls reach the provider and never touch the engine — the fake provider answers the opposite of what the engine would, which is what gives the assertions discriminating power; an unforwarded owner still uses the engine; provider failures propagate verbatim with no engine fallback; the permission gate precedes forwarding); mobile `cargo test --no-fail-fast` all green (no mobile change); root `pnpm exec eslint .` 0 errors; host clippy no new warnings (54 pre-existing on `--lib`, none in the touched ranges); rustfmt clean on every file this ticket touched (the remaining host-tree drift is in other in-flight files)
- Not run: `cross-end-tests` (no cross-end protocol change — capability routing is host-internal), the wasm app builds and `gen/android` Kotlin (no plugin or Kotlin change), and the reverse-direction mutation of the closed-table lock (the forward direction was verified: deleting one export from the routable table turns the lock red with the counts; the reverse direction is a straight scan over the same data)

#### Build: sccache introduced as the repo-wide Rust compile cache (root `.cargo/config.toml` `rustc-wrapper` + `[env]`, local and CI)

- **What changed**: `[build] rustc-wrapper = "sccache"` in the repo-root cargo config — effective for every crate (both hosts, wasm apps, fixtures, server-libs, host-kits, cross-end) via the cwd ancestor-chain config merge — plus `[env] SCCACHE_CACHE_SIZE = { value = "20GiB", force = false }`. sccache caches rustc compilation by content hash, independent of the target dirs, so two recurring costs are gone: (1) the host targets' 15 GiB threshold `cargo clean` no longer forces a full dependency rebuild (measured on `bedcode-host-kit`: full wasmtime compile 3m34s → clean + cache-hit rebuild 37s, 244/244 hits); (2) the 8 governed target locations no longer compile their shared dependency graphs once each (wasmtime in server-libs vs host-kits, cross-end's union graph)
- **Coexists with incremental compilation** (this was the 2026-09-26 decision-record reason for rejecting sccache): cargo enables incremental only for local crates (`-Zincremental`); sccache passes those through uncached and caches the full compiles of dependencies — dev iteration speed is unchanged
- **CI is a hard dependency now**: every cargo-invoking job (`test.yml` rust-desktop/rust-mobile, `release.yml` build-windows/build-android/build-linux/package-plugins/package-sdks, `sdk-publish.yml` verify/publish-crates) installs sccache via `mozilla-actions/sccache-action@v0.0.11` — required because the root config forces the wrapper (missing sccache = cargo fails loudly, no silent fallback). CI does not enable the sccache GHA-cache backend (cross-run caching stays with `Swatinem/rust-cache`); job-level `SCCACHE_CACHE_SIZE=2GiB` overrides the root default via `force=false`
- **Install**: `~/.cargo/bin/sccache` (v0.18.0) on Linux/macOS from the mozilla/sccache GitHub release; `scoop install sccache` on Windows. Cache at `~/.cache/sccache` (clean: `sccache --stop-server && rm -rf ~/.cache/sccache`). Docs: `docs/knowledge/build-process.md` "sccache 编译缓存" section, AGENTS §2

#### Desktop: the peer-network capability domain (host-peer, 19 primitives) moved out of the kernel — and a reverse dependency removed — into `bedcode-server-peer-net` (no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **What moved**: the whole guest-facing binding layer — the session handle table (`sess-<uuid>` mint / resolve / take / owner restore), the data-plane auto-redial (a handle-remembered endpoint re-runs the engine handshake and retries once when the engine reports a missing discovery-cache entry), the permission-gate position, and payload validation. The host module that held it went from 727 lines to 112. All 19 `host_peer_*` primitive names, 20 wire strings, and both structured log sites (the owner-rejection `plugin_id`/`handle` warn, the auto-redial `node_id` info) are preserved verbatim
- **This ticket does one thing more than the last: it removes a reverse dependency.** The binding layer had **20** call sites that fetched engine state "from the host assembly surface", which required the capability crate to know the host's `AppHandle` and managed-state table — exactly the direction ADR 0022's trim line removes. They now ask a port for an already-assembled engine context, taking the binding layer's references to the host assembly surface to **0**; the assembly itself (pulling the four engine handles out of the runtime state table) stays in the host adapter, still the only place in the repo that knows `AppHandle`. **The peer-network engine itself is untouched**
- **Three boundary ports**: permission gate (host security gate, reusing the existing decision and its warn path), engine context, and the sync↔async bridge — which must remain the host's single implementation, since it carries the actix `current_thread` self-deadlock workaround and the ambient runtime. The headless-context wire string `peer-net unavailable in headless context (no app_handle)` is preserved verbatim and is now a constant owned by the capability domain that the host references, so there is one source of truth on the domain side
- **Two judgement orders are locked behaviour and must not be swapped**: (1) on `close`, a non-owner gets the owner rejection rather than "unavailable in headless context" first, otherwise a privilege probe's result becomes non-deterministic; (2) `send-files`' detection of the v31-retired `concurrency` pulse field (present ⇒ explicit error naming the rebuild) runs before handle resolution — it is the behaviour-level fail-visible half of the transfer-orchestration downsink
- **The host now assembles it instead of hardcoding it**: the crate is shaped like the `host-websocket` one — `HostModule` + `inventory::submit!`, installed through the capability-module registry in one pass. The host changes are: one whitelist entry, one forced-reference line, deletion of this domain's `Host` impl and its `add_to_linker` line, and one line at each of the three assembly points (production / headless test rig / PluginHost test harness)
- **The crate's own dependency-direction lock did its job on the first pass**: it rejects host-path literals even inside comments. The initial implementation wrote bookkeeping sentences like "migrated from host module X" into the doc comments and the lock went red on 14 of them. The fix was to **reword the prose, not relax the lock** — that is what the lock is for
- **Verification**: `bedcode-server-peer-net` 48 green (31 baseline + 17 = 9 migrated verbatim + 8 new: headless wording locked verbatim, the owner-reaches-engine positive case, a non-owner's close leaving the handle registered, the retired-field error, both payload shapes clearing validation, invalid-JSON wording, unknown-handle wording, owner check preceding payload parsing, and "collect-outgoing is the one primitive that needs no engine context"); desktop `cargo test`: `--lib` 874 passed / 1 failed / 1 ignored (the one failure is the pre-existing stale `session_e2e` assertion; accounting = last ticket's 885 − 9 migrated = 876 ✅ exact match); `capabilities_lock` 7, `hot_path_logging_lock` 3, `server_integration` / `ws_auth_rules` / `broadcast_shutdown` / `http_auth_biometric` / `pty_session_chain` / `build_manifest_smoke` 1 each; `wasm_bridge_bench` both magnitude gates PASS (real WASM plugin loaded through the new two-stage assembly: nop round trip 64.4µs < 3000µs, bus binary throughput 102.9 MiB/s > 1 MiB/s)
- Not run: `cross-end-tests` (no cross-end protocol change) and the wasm app build (no plugin-side change). Three pre-existing warnings on the peer engine side (dead field / dead fn / one no-op `drop(&T)`) are left alone per "do not touch the engine" — what that `drop` was meant to achieve needs an engine-side decision and deserves its own ticket

#### Desktop: the WebSocket capability domain (host-websocket, 15 primitives) moved out of the kernel into `bedcode-server-websocket` (no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **What moved**: the whole guest-facing binding layer — outbound connection table (owner-scoped handles, `wss://` refused explicitly, per-connection bounded send queue), inbound plugin endpoints (shape/auth validation, send/broadcast/kick/unregister/list, de-identified `connection-context`), the frame reader/writer tasks, the `events-ws` dispatch fallback accounting, owner-scoped status events, and plugin-deactivation reclamation. The host module that held it went from 1126 lines to 156. Signatures, return shapes, error strings and structured log fields are preserved verbatim; the section comments (client domain / server domain / reclaim / reader-writer / frame dispatch) travelled with the code
- **The host now assembles it instead of hardcoding it**: `bedcode-server-websocket` implements `HostModule` + `inventory::submit!`, so `add_to_linker` picks it up through the capability-module registry (whitelisted, verified both ways). Adding the crate as a dependency plus one whitelist entry is the whole integration — no host wiring edits. Adding the crate's new module descriptor to the whitelist (`websocket`) is the only host-side change
- **Five boundary ports instead of a reverse dependency**: permission gate (host-side security gate, reusing the existing `check_permission` and its warn path), owner-scoped event publishing, the `BusPort` object handed to endpoint registration, `events-ws` frame dispatch (three-state result), and the sync↔async bridge — the bridge must be the host's single implementation, since it carries the actix `current_thread` self-deadlock workaround and the ambient runtime; the capability crate would otherwise carry a second copy
- **New in the mechanism kernel: per-instance domain ports.** A capability crate's `impl … Host for WasmPluginState` cannot reach the host context (downcasting is forbidden by design), so it takes ports from a process-level singleton — but one process can hold several host contexts (headless tests build one per case; the two-plugin WS isolation case builds two with different grants). First-install-wins would have made the domain read another context's permission manager and bus. `HostPorts::domain_ports(domain)` (default `None`, opaque `Arc<dyn Any>`, kit still names no domain port type) plus `WasmHostContext::set_domain_ports` binds ports to the instance; the domain asks the instance first and falls back to the process-level slot. Tickets 05 / 06 / 08 reuse this channel
- **A pre-existing gap surfaced by this regression**: the `PluginHost` test harness builds the struct literal directly and never ran the capability-port assembly chain, so `deactivate_plugin` → owner reclamation → "ports not installed" panic. Two lines in the harness now mirror production
- **Verification**: `bedcode-server-websocket` 47 green (25 in the moved domain = 23 migrated verbatim + 2 new: the three-state frame dispatch only counts the not-exported case, and frame-target labels cover both domains); `bedcode-host-kit` 11 green; desktop `cargo test --lib` 883 passed / 1 failed (the one failure is the pre-existing stale `session_e2e` assertion — the plugin now emits a fourth task domain; accounting: ticket-03 baseline 906 − 23 migrated = 883); `capabilities_lock` 7, `hot_path_logging_lock` 3 (its site table now points at the crate path), `server_integration` / `ws_auth_rules` / `broadcast_shutdown` / `http_auth_biometric` / `pty_session_chain` / `build_manifest_smoke` 1 each; `wasm_bridge_bench` both magnitude gates PASS (real WASM plugin loaded through the new two-stage assembly: nop round trip 68.0µs < 3000µs, bus binary throughput 101.9 MiB/s > 1 MiB/s); terminal-session 449 green (it consumes the crate as a dev-dependency for its cross-end contract tests)
- Not run: host `cargo clippy --lib` (disk had 1.7GiB left after two full-partition fills; a full rebuild would hit the `Bus error` linker failure — the crate's clippy is clean and the host build shows zero warnings in the six touched files) and `cross-end-tests` (no cross-end protocol change)

#### Sidebar order + device-pairing / session tab bars aligned to Agent Tasks + Agent Hub "Apply to" secondary page rebuilt (UI only, no ABI / WIT / protocol change)

- **Sidebar: Agent Hub now sits directly below Agent Tasks** (slot `240` → `215`). Ordering has a single source of truth: the host `useSidebarMenu` merges built-in items and every plugin-contributed view and sorts ascending by `order`; `240` encoded the older "right after file-transfer" convention, which does not match the desired `Agent Tasks → Agent Hub` sequence. `215` takes a free gap between the multiples of ten (`200` sessions / `210` agent tasks / `220` file-transfer / `230` ai-chatbox). Both sources must agree — `plugin.json`'s `contributes.views[0].order` and the frontend `registerSidebarPanel({ order })` — because the frontend one drives the sidebar menu while host Rust `register_views` registers the very same views from the manifest; drift desynchronises the two lists with **no runtime error at all**. The new `sidebarOrder.test.ts` locks that dual-source agreement plus the slot placement
- **The tab bars on device pairing / sessions were not navigation, they were a pair of buttons.** Both used an `inline-flex` shrink-to-fit strip floating on the page background (measured: 172px / 214px total, 80px per item) with nothing separating them from the toolbar header, so they read as a couple of pills hanging off the body text. They now follow the Agent Tasks (`TaskHistoryView`) spec: a full-width segmented control (`flex` container + `flex-1` items), with the tab band itself carrying a bottom hairline and the same `--bg-card` as the header above it (a two-tier header with one divider). The sessions view additionally caps its tab strip with `max-w-5xl mx-auto` — that page's content column is width-limited, so an uncapped strip is wider than its content on wide screens
- **Agent Hub "Apply to" secondary page**: the page replaces the whole provider list view, so both "how do I get back" and "what am I writing" have to be answered by the panel itself
  - The exit changed from a corner `×` to a page-header "← Back to providers". A `×` reads as *close a dialog*, not *go back a level*; when the page's only exit is not labelled "back", users cannot tell which level they are on
  - A **preset summary row** was added to the header (dialect / Base URL / model count / key mask). Those decision inputs exist on the list row and were all lost on entry, leaving users to rely on memory
  - Field hints moved *inside* their own field block (they used to sit outside, so the page read as one wall of text), and the action row is separated from the form: top hairline, "will write N CLI(s)" echo on the left, primary action on the right
  - The content column is capped at 760px (`--ah-pv-apply-measure`, shared by the form body and the action row) so a 1150px-wide card no longer hosts a 1150px-wide text input
- **Verification**: desktop `vitest run` full suite 1583 passed / 115 files (including 2 new `sidebarOrder` cases and 6 new `A1-c` cases in `components.test.ts`); terminal-session 244 passed, agent-hub 508 passed; root `pnpm exec eslint .` 0 errors (all 115 warnings pre-existing); `vue-tsc` matches the baseline (agent-hub 2 pre-existing StatsTab errors, terminal-session 41 pre-existing errors, none in the files touched here); labels neither wrap nor overflow at five viewport widths (1600 / 1280 / 1024 / 860 / 700); verified on both themes × both locales
- Not run: `pnpm run build` (with wasmHash injection) and the wasm artifact build — only plugin frontend templates / CSS / i18n and the view slot in `plugin.json` changed, no Rust involved. No Rust / WIT / ABI / cross-end protocol change, so `cargo test` and `cross-end-tests` were not run

#### Desktop: page transitions unified into one system, with four switchable effects and no more black-flash

- **Root cause of the black screen / flicker on every page switch**: all view swaps used `mode="out-in"`, which finishes the leave animation *before* mounting the enter. In between, the transition container holds nothing and shows `--bg-page` — `#15130f` / `#0f172a` / `#101713` / `#0b1620` / `#1b1210` in the five dark themes, i.e. near-black. On a full-viewport swap that reads as a black flash, not a transition (the same mechanism had already produced a *permanent* white screen on plugin views, fixed separately in 2026-09-25)
- **Fix**: the default overlapping mode is used instead, and `.page-leave-active` sets `position: absolute` (padded containers compensate via `--page-swap-pad-*`), so the outgoing view leaves the document flow while the incoming one occupies it from frame one — the container is never empty
- **Unification**: page-level transitions were defined five times over with drifting values (host `page`, `ah-page`, `ft-page`, three same-named-but-different `tab-fade`, `page-fade`, `view-slide`; 0.12s–0.26s, 2px–14px, mixed easings). There is now one definition in `src/style.css` (`.page-*` + `.page-swap`), durations/easings/offsets come from `--motion-page-*` tokens, and the transition name is always `page`
- **Four effects, one switch point**: `fade` / `slide-up` (default) / `slide-left` / `zoom`, selected by `PAGE_TRANSITION_EFFECT` in `src/utils/pageTransition.ts` (code-level only, no settings UI by design — page motion is app-level look, not a user preference). An unknown name throws instead of silently falling back
- **Enforcement**: `src/__tests__/style/pageTransition.test.ts` locks the name, the absence of `out-in`, the container class, effect coverage, the `prefers-reduced-motion` fallback, and that the four base classes are defined only in `src/style.css`
- Docs: `frontend-styles/ANIMATIONS.md` updated to the unified system

#### Agent Hub providers: codex is now an apply target (register provider + set current model, key via env var name)

- **The reason it was closed was "format pending calibration", not "format unsupported"** (`.scratch/2026-09-13-agent-hub/spec.md`, open question 3). Calibrating against a real installation turned up three hard constraints that dictated the shape of this implementation
- **codex has no concept of a model list.** pi uses `providers.<n>.models[]`, opencode uses `provider.<n>.models{}`; codex has only the **global scalars** `model` / `model_provider`. Measured: writing `models = [...]` inside `[model_providers.<n>]` is refused outright by codex (`unknown configuration field`, and `--strict-config` rejects it too; the `totally_bogus_key` control hits the same rejection path, so this is not a syntax mistake). Applying a provider to codex therefore degrades to **registering the provider and pointing the current model at the preset's first model** — the strongest thing codex can express, not a shortcut in the implementation
- **codex only speaks Responses.** Measured: `wire_api = "chat"` → `no longer supported`, with **not a single request sent** (the `responses` control posts to `/v1/responses` normally). anthropic / gemini styles have no codex form, so they are refused explicitly (`reason = "unsupportedDialect"`) rather than written as a config guaranteed to fail; openai / custom always write `wire_api = "responses"`
- **Credentials are indirect.** codex's config carries only an **environment variable name** (`env_key = "TOKENPLAN_API_KEY"`); the value comes from the user's shell environment. This implementation **only writes the name** and never touches the user's env files or shell config. The name defaults to one derived from the preset name (`InkStone` → `INKSTONE_API_KEY`; names starting with a digit such as "360 网关" get an underscore prefix, since they would be illegal), and the user can change it to whatever the vendor's docs specify
- **New TOML writing layer** (`providers/codex.rs`), still a text-level splice (no whole-file deserialize-and-rewrite), preserving `#` comments and hand-written content byte for byte. Three structural constraints are documented at the module head: ① top-level keys may only appear **before the first table header** (after it, a key is absorbed into some table and silently corrupts the user's config — `set_top_level` sidesteps this by construction); ② appending `[model_providers.x]` is always legal, but editing an existing table must stay inside its span (up to the next header); ③ replacing a whole line drops that line's trailing comment (acceptable for the controlled fields). The quoted headers found in a real `config.toml` (`[projects."/path/..."]`, `[hooks.state."/x/y.json:session_start:0:0"]`, with `/ . :` inside the key) are pinned as test fixtures
- **The destructive part is previewed.** Writing the provider table only registers it; taking effect also means changing the global `model` / `model_provider`, which **overrides the model the user is currently on**. The panel shows "current → will switch to" before writing (and shows nothing when the target is the same model — no change, no noise). A codex-only apply hides the key-mode selector entirely (codex takes no key value), which also stops "inline selected but empty" from failing an otherwise valid apply
- **Verified**: 13 new codex cases (TOML splice + plan guards), including "a replace must not be written as an insert" — the first version did exactly that and `replaces_keys_inside_existing_table_only` turned red; agent-hub `cargo test` 202 green, vitest 498 green (target selection, editable env name, switch preview, no preview when unchanged, dialect refusal, per-target reason copy); root `eslint` 0 errors (0 warnings in agent-hub); `vue-tsc` matches baseline; `rustfmt` / `clippy` clean on my files; `pnpm run build` passes including wasmHash injection

#### Agent Hub providers: apply to several CLIs at once, query the model list, and stop silently writing a zero-model provider

- **Reproduced defect: the receipt said `applied: true` while pi showed zero models.** The InkStone preset's `models_json` was an empty array, and `merge_pi_entry` only writes a `models` field when the list is non-empty — so `~/.pi/agent/models.json` gained a provider entry holding nothing but `api` / `baseUrl`. pi's `/model` lists a provider's models, hence "configured successfully, no models at all". Nothing on the write path checked for this, and the receipt reported success either way
- **The root cause is not the write, it is that nothing could populate the model list.** The preset editor had one manually typed textarea and no way to fetch one, so an empty list saved happily. There are now two complementary paths: ① an **optional model query URL** (new `models_url` column, idempotent v3 migration; derivable from the base URL as `{base}/models`, and freely editable for gateways that put `/models` elsewhere) — "fetch models" renders candidates as chips, and clicking one or "add all" writes them into **the same textarea** (hand-typed entries are never overwritten); ② **manual input stays exactly as it was**, so a failed fetch (no `/models`, auth required, network down) costs nothing. The new `agent-hub.fetch-models` command goes through the existing `host-http` primitive (behind the `network:http` declaration gate and the outbound authorization gate); response shapes cover `data[].id` (OpenAI/Anthropic), `models[].name` (Gemini, `models/` prefix stripped) and a bare array. An **unrecognized shape fails visibly instead of returning an empty list** — an empty list is exactly what the apply flow would write through as "this provider really has no models"
- **Fail-visible: a merged entry with zero models is no longer written.** pi / opencode targets compute the post-merge model count first and refuse with `reason = "noModels"` (an existing entry that already carries models is still allowed, since model lists merge by id and keep user definitions); claude is exempt (its env block only changes endpoint and token). The apply panel blocks it one step earlier and names the affected targets, and the preset list tags model-less presets with a warning marker
- **Apply targets went from single-select to multi-select**: claude / pi / opencode can be written in one pass, in selection order. `apply-provider` now takes a `targets` array (the legacy scalar `target` still works) and **a failing target no longer aborts the others** — the receipt carries per-target `results[]` (`ok` / `files` / `reason`), the panel renders one line per target, failure classes go through i18n (`noModels` / bridge conflict / write failed), and the guest's raw error text only reaches the log
- **Also fixed a silent protocol downgrade**: the preset's apiStyle only expresses the "openai family", yet re-applying rewrote an existing `openai-responses` (pi) or `@ai-sdk/openai` (opencode) entry down to completions / openai-compatible — swapping an endpoint the user had already tuned for a different one, with a very hard-to-spot symptom (it connects, it just behaves differently). Same-family dialects are now preserved; cross-family styles (anthropic / gemini) still switch as the preset asks. New entries also get a `name` display field, which re-applying an imported preset used to blank out
- **Verified**: plugin `cargo test` 189 green (new cases for dialect preservation, multi-target parsing, model-shape parsing and the size cap); agent-hub vitest 484 green (new cases for multi-select, per-target outcomes, the zero-model warning, fetch success/failure and hand-typed entries surviving "add all"); root `eslint` 0 errors (0 warnings in agent-hub); `vue-tsc` matches baseline (2 pre-existing StatsTab errors); `rustfmt` / `clippy` clean on my files; `pnpm run build` passes end to end (wasmHash injection included)

#### Build outputs: two `.cargo/config.toml` files pointed one level too high, splitting each shared target directory in two
