- **The bug produced no error at all**: cargo resolves a config file's `target-dir` **relative to the parent of the `.cargo` directory**, and both desktop configs had one `..` too many. `bedcode-desktop/wasm-apps/.cargo/config.toml` (`../../target/wasm-apps`) actually resolved to the **repository root** `target/wasm-apps`, and `bedcode-desktop/packages/.cargo/config.toml` (`../../target/fixtures`) to the repository root `target/fixtures` — so `pnpm run build` artifacts (which pass `--target-dir` from the app root) and `cargo test` artifacts (which pick up the config) landed in **two different directories**: 478M and 7.5G measured side by side. Every comment, `AGENTS.md` §3 and `docs/knowledge/build-process.md` claimed a single directory; only `cargo metadata`'s `target_directory` field told the truth
- **Fixed to the documented locations**: `../target/wasm-apps` and `../target/fixtures`, which resolve to `bedcode-desktop/target/wasm-apps` / `bedcode-desktop/target/fixtures` — the same paths `WASM_TARGET_DIR` (`scripts/plugin-wasm-config.mjs`) and `fixture_target.rs::dir()` already used. Verified with `cargo metadata` on a wasm-app crate, a fixture crate, and the server-lib crates
- **Documented the trap that hid it**: the same string `../../target/wasm-apps` means different things in the two places it appears — the CLI `--target-dir` is resolved against the **process cwd**, the config-file `target-dir` against the **`.cargo` parent**. They coincide only because the bases differ by exactly the number of `..` written. `AGENTS.md` §3 and `build-process.md` now state the base explicitly and require a `cargo metadata` check after any `target-dir` edit
- **An undocumented落点 is now documented**: `target/server-libs` at the repository root (6 crates: `bedcode-server-base` / `-core` / `-http` / `-websocket` / `-peer-net` + `bedcode-crypto-engine`, redirected by their own `.cargo/config.toml`) existed but appeared in no table; added to `build-process.md`, `AGENTS.md` §3, and to `check-target-size.js`'s `rootTargetDirs` so `pnpm run target:size` reports it
- **Reclaimed**: the orphaned repository-root `target/wasm-apps` (7.5G) plus `cross-end-tests/target` (21G) were deleted — 95% → 78% disk usage

#### Mobile: terminal-link backoff converged onto the shared policy, plus link-liveness (half-open) detection and visible reconnection

- **The terminal link's backoff now shares `ReconnectManager`** with the device-level event channel (entry below). `terminal_link.rs` previously carried its own hand-written table, `RECONNECT_BASE_MS = 500` / `RECONNECT_MAX_MS = 8000`, with two problems: ① `500` sits **below** the global floor `MIN_RECONNECT_DELAY_MS = 1000` — the exact "no backoff floor" shape behind the 2026-09-29 self-heal storm (616 attempts in 98 seconds); ② no jitter, so N sessions reconnect in lockstep. `link_io` now drives the shared policy (`max_retries = 0` keeps the existing "retry forever" semantics — the link lives and dies with its subscribe lifecycle and never adjudicates "N failures, hand back to the user"). The policy construction was extracted into `terminal_reconnect_policy()` so the backoff sequence can be asserted directly — **inlined inside `link_io` it is untestable, and "did someone hand-write another table again" would be answerable only by reading code, which is how this defect arose**
- **Cadence change (a known cost of converging)**: 500→1000→…→8000 capped becomes 1000→2000→…→30000 capped. A more conservative first attempt and a longer ceiling
- **The terminal link had no heartbeat at all**; a dead connection could only be discovered via `ws_rx.next()` returning `Err` or a `Close` frame. On a half-open socket (peer process dead, an intermediate NAT still holding the TCP connection) `next()` hangs forever, and the `ack_idle_tick` branch cannot save it — that branch only flushes acks, and `ws_tx.send` still succeeds on a half-open connection. Result: when the desktop crashed or the phone switched WiFi→4G, the user got a terminal that "looked alive but never printed". Now it reuses `HeartbeatManager` (the same predicates as the event channel): `mark_connected()` after the handshake sets the baseline, a standard `WsMsg::Ping` goes out on an interval, **any inbound frame** calls `on_activity()` to refresh the baseline, and a timeout is declared dead and falls back onto the existing `Err(Io)` reconnect path
- **`on_activity()` is new semantics**: recognising Pong alone would classify "the terminal is idle" as "the connection is dead" — a silent shell produces no Pong whatsoever, only business output frames
- **Reconnection is now visible to users** (previously the `reconnecting` event only reached `logger.debug` and the terminal froze silently — the Severity High "No feedback" anti-pattern flagged in the ui-ux-pro-max ux domain): `SessionBuffer` gains `reconnecting` / `reconnectInMs` (`phase=connecting` cannot distinguish "first subscribe" from "backing off after a drop", and those mean opposite things to the user, so they need separate state); Rust emits a new `reconnect_scheduled` event carrying `retry_in_ms` (backoff caps at 30s, so without a countdown users cannot tell "reconnecting" from "dead"); `TerminalView` renders a non-blocking banner at the top of the terminal output area (token-bound, mirroring the existing `loading-*` blueprint; `pointer-events: none` so it does not also break xterm's native copy/selection), with the countdown ticking locally at 1s granularity (Rust only emits once per scheduled round, so a store-level timer would keep background sessions alive)
- **Filled in the "auto-reconnect is off" notice**: when `auto_reconnect` is false, Rust tags `ws_unexpected_disconnect` with `auto_reconnect_disabled`; previously the supervisor just logged a line and broke, the UI showed a generic "connection lost", and users could not tell "recovering" from "nobody is coming" — they had to sit through 3 backoff rounds plus the auth timeout to find out
- **Verified**: mobile `cargo test` 365 green (+8 new: terminal backoff matches the shared sequence round by round, never breaches the global floor, is capped, keeps its unlimited-retry contract; liveness baseline after `mark_connected` with no Pong, any inbound frame refreshing the baseline, `mark_connected` reset, and no verdict before the loop starts); `pnpm run test:run` fully green (terminalBuffer +6: flag set, countdown carried, both withdrawn on returning to live, no false positive during first subscribe, no invented number when `retry_in_ms` is absent, no inheritance into a fresh buffer; connection-flow +1 for the auto-reconnect-off notice); `cross-end-tests` green except mDNS (whose own panic message documents "cannot be verified where the host/container has no multicast" — reproduced identically with this change stashed, so it is unrelated); root `eslint` 0 errors; `cargo clippy` warning count matches baseline
- **Four mutations, each turning the suite red**: terminal backoff reverted to the hand-written 500ms base (caught by the convergence lock), `on_activity` no longer refreshing the baseline (caught by the half-open lock), removing the `reconnecting` assignment (4 tests red), removing the `auto_reconnect_disabled` branch (1 test red). **The backoff's "never breaches the floor" and "is capped" tests stay green under the 500ms mutation** — they guard invariants (guaranteed by the `MIN_RECONNECT_DELAY_MS` clamp), not configuration identity; the sequence-equality test is the one that catches configuration drift

#### Mobile WS reconnect: one strategy owner, real half-open detection, and a three-way close-code split

- **The backoff engine was written but never wired**: `ReconnectManager` (605 lines — exponential backoff, 10% jitter, same-cause circuit breaker, 1s floor clamp) hung off `WsClient::reconnect()`, which had **zero callers** (`rg "\.reconnect\(\)"` finds nothing). What actually ran was the hardcoded `DEFAULT_RETRY_DELAYS_MS = [1s, 2s, 4s]` table inside `ConnectionManager::reconnect` — **no jitter, no floor clamp** — and its third entry (4000ms) was never even indexed, since the loop runs 3 rounds. Two tables with identical contents from different sources, held together only by a comment claiming they "stay consistent"
- **The irony**: the `MIN_RECONNECT_DELAY_MS = 1000` guard added after the 2026-09-29 self-heal storm (616 attempts in 98 seconds ≈ 6.3 Hz) lives inside that dead code, so it did nothing for the real storm; the real path's user-settable interval bottomed out at exactly 1s, coinciding with the clamp line and rendering it moot
- **P0: two strategies ran concurrently and corrupted each other's counters.** A single disconnect woke both the Rust `EventWsSupervisor` (self-heal) and the frontend `handleUnexpectedDisconnect` (fixed interval, max 3, recursive retry). Rust usually won (first round waits 0ms; the frontend waited 5s by default), and the frontend's later `ws_reconnect` hit `is_reconnecting` and skipped — but its counter had **already been incremented**, so all 3 retries were burned on no-ops. Symptom: "the desktop is online, yet the app says auto-reconnect gave up"
- **Fix: a single source of truth for backoff.** `ConnectionManager` now owns `reconnect_policy`, and its top-level loop drives it (`start` / `on_failure` / `on_success` / `reset` / `is_abandoned`). The wait moved to the end of each failed round — `current_delay` is naturally the current round's value, so no index offset is needed, and early exits (no target / no token) no longer burn a backoff slot. `WsClient::reconnect()` and the `DEFAULT_RETRY_DELAYS_MS` table are deleted, leaving one source. Jitter and the floor clamp thereby reach the real path, at unchanged cadence (immediate first try, then 1s, 2s ± 10%)
- **The frontend no longer owns a reconnect loop**: `handleUnexpectedDisconnect` is gone (recursive retry, `MAX_AUTO_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS`, `autoReconnectAttemptCount` all retired); the frontend only subscribes to `ws_reconnecting` / `ws_reconnected` / `ws_reconnect_failed` to update UI. `autoReconnectAborted` is now purely a **stale-event discard flag**
- **Half-open detection was entirely non-functional**: for the whole window before the first Pong (a full heartbeat interval, 30s by default), `last_pong` stayed `None` and `is_connection_lost` returned `false` for `None`. Nothing in that window could fire — `send(Ping)` succeeds on a half-open socket (it only writes to the local buffer) and the timeout check was disabled — so a dead connection could hang indefinitely. New `mark_connected()` (called once after a successful handshake) gives the verdict a baseline: `last_pong` if a Pong arrived, otherwise the connect moment
- **Close codes went from two buckets to three**: previously only "auth-fatal (4001/4003) → don't reconnect" vs "everything else → reconnect", so protocol errors (1002/1003/1010), non-UTF-8 payloads (1007), policy rejections (1008) and oversized messages (1009) were all retried three times as if they were network jitter. New `WS_NON_RETRYABLE_CLOSE_CODES` + `is_non_retryable_close_code`, honoured by both the supervisor and ConnMonitor; 1000/1001/1005/1006/1011/1012/1013 stay explicitly **retryable** (graceful shutdown, crash, overload, network switch all deserve self-healing), and unknown business codes default to retryable (fail-open on retry, the opposite of auth's fail-closed)
- **The third bucket needs its own wording**: the `ws_unexpected_disconnect` payload gained `non_retryable` (old ends ignore unknown fields), and the toast now says "update the app" rather than "re-pair" — otherwise users re-pair in a loop against a version/protocol mismatch that re-pairing cannot fix
- **The auto-reconnect switch now reaches the connection layer**: new `set_auto_reconnect` command + `ConnectionManager::auto_reconnect` flag. The Rust flag does not restore itself from localStorage, so without this sync a switch the user turned off silently flips back on after a restart; both `init()` and the settings page `onMounted` push idempotently
- **The "reconnect interval" setting was removed**: backoff is exponential with jitter, so exposing the algorithm's parameters only invites users to configure a self-DoS against their own desktop; leaving the control in place while it no longer does anything is silent failure. Removed from the settings page, the `MobileSettings` type, and both zh-CN / en locale files (stale localStorage fields are simply ignored)
- **Verified**: mobile `cargo test --lib` 357 green (+10 new: half-open regression locks ×3, close-code three-way positive/negative/boundary ×5, auto-reconnect flag shared-state ×3; one existing test that had locked in the old silent behaviour was rewritten); full `cargo test` green; `pnpm run test:run` 539 green across 54 files (3 connection-flow integration tests rewritten, 4 added, covering "the frontend must not initiate reconnect" and the third bucket's mutually-exclusive wording); `cross-end-tests` fully green (14 test binaries); root `eslint` 0 errors; `cargo clippy` warnings 47 → 47 (none added); rustfmt leaves only pre-existing drift in 23 files, none on the lines added here
- **Left open**: `CIRCUIT_BREAKER_SAME_CAUSE_LIMIT = 5` is unreachable under `DEFAULT_MAX_RETRIES = 3` (rounds exhaust first) and is now defence-in-depth. Making it bite needs the threshold lowered to ≤3 — a product default, so it awaits confirmation. The terminal-stream WS connection still has no supervisor (`WsClient::new` has exactly one production call site, the event channel); who establishes it and who reconnects it needs separate confirmation

#### Desktop Auto Task: the retrying state and every session-scoped task now reach a terminal state (closed loop)

- **The reported symptom was real and reproducible**: a task stuck in `retrying` with its queue item stuck in `executing` and the next queued task never dispatched. Inspecting the shipped plugin database found the actual row — status `retrying`, last event `2026-10-03 16:10:57`, `completed_at` NULL, still `retrying` 8+ hours later, with the sibling queue item still `pending` and the session itself already gone. `retrying` means "the agent is auto-retrying a recoverable error" and is supposed to converge within minutes (pi retries at most `maxRetries=3` times with backoff capped at 60s), so a long silence means **the converging signal was lost**, not "the task is still running"
- **Host side had no bound on that state**: the only backstop was the 12h executing-silence watchdog keyed on the queue row, and a host restart left *everything* dangling — `activate` clears the session table (sessions and PTYs are process-scoped) but nothing reconciled the task rows, so the row stayed `retrying` forever, `has_active_task` stayed true and the whole queue stalled (the 12h watchdog would have caught it hours later, i.e. effectively never)
- **Three closure gaps closed** (no WIT / ABI / HTTP / wire change, all inside the terminal-session plugin):
  - **retrying watchdog** (new `queue-retrying-check` tick domain): a task row silent in `retrying` for longer than **20 min** (far above pi's few-minute retry ceiling, far below the 12h watchdog) is converged to `interrupted` with a readable reason, **and the queue continues** (`try_dispatch_next` → next task runs). Grouped per session so one stalled row can never interrupt the task just dispatched after it
  - **startup reconciliation** (`state::recover_running_tasks_on_restart`, called at activate next to the existing scheduled-job recovery): running task rows (`in_progress` / `asking` / `retrying`) and un-terminated queue items (`pending` / `waiting` / `executing`) of sessions that are no longer registered all become `interrupted`, with the reason "Host restarted while task was running". Orphaned `pending` items are converged too — their session is gone, so there is nowhere to deliver them
  - **session remove / restart**: both摘-record before killing the PTY, so the exit event no-ops and the session-end backstop never ran; the task domain now converges in-flight tasks *before* the record is removed. Restart keeps `pending` items (the new session with the same id picks them up on idle)
- **Root cause in the hook adapters**: pi's own kernel checks a **non-retryable limit/quota table first** (`insufficient_quota`, `quota exceeded`, `billing`, …) and gives up immediately, while the shipped adapters only checked the *retryable* patterns — so `429 {"code":"insufficient_quota"}` matched `\b429\b` and was pushed as `retrying` even though no retry was ever attempted. Both the pi and opencode adapters now carry the same non-retryable guard (limit-class errors are left alone; the terminal state still comes from the agent's own settled event, so the two never diverge). Template versions bumped (pi 6→7, opencode 4→5) so already-deployed copies are rewritten
- **Convergence SQL has one source of truth**: both convergence sites now build their `IN (...)` predicate from `RUNNING_STATUSES` / `UNTERMINATED_QUEUE_STATUSES` instead of inline literals — dropping `retrying` from one of the two copies is exactly the drift that produced this bug
- **Verified**: plugin crate `cargo test --lib` 449 green (+14 new: watchdog verdict positive/negative/boundary, threshold two-sided bounds, converge-and-continue shape, retrying-only scope, per-session grouping, status-set coverage, activate wiring order, hook guard presence); 4 mutations (threshold `>`→`>=`, dropping `GROUP BY`, dropping the continue-dispatch, dropping `retrying` from the status set) each turned the suite red; `wasm32-wasip3` build clean with no new warnings (18 pre-existing, none in the touched code)

#### Desktop Auto Task: config-keyed task queues (enqueue first, session on start), auto-mode session rotation, and terminal windows closing with their session

- **Session picker is now "Select Execution Session"** — besides running sessions, each supported session config adds a **"Create new {config name} session"** option. Submitting a task there does **not create a session immediately**: the task joins a **config-keyed task queue** (reusing the config's existing unstarted queue, or creating one), and the session is only created when the queue is started or auto mode triggers it (tasks are held in `task_queue` under the queue id as the session key, then migrated to the real session id on start — reusing the existing queue engine)
- **Task Queues section (new, in the Current Tasks tab)**: each queue card shows its execution session (config name) and the queued auto-task list, plus **Start** (create the session and execute the batch) and **Auto mode** (when on, a queue with tasks creates its session and executes automatically); after start the batch moves into the per-session queue view (the toggle becomes the session's auto-execute flag); **not-yet-executed tasks can be edited inline** (prompt rewrite via the existing queue-update, and the same inline edit covers pending items in the per-session Executing section)
- **Presets pick their target queue**: the preset "Add to Queue" action now writes into a selected task queue (`preset-enqueue-queue`)
- **Auto mode rotates sessions between tasks**: when a task finishes, the session is closed and a new one with the same config executes the next task (existing `rotate_session_for_next_task`). While closing, **desktop terminal windows follow along** — `TerminalWindowView` subscribes to `session:stopped` and closes itself when its session id matches (restart is unaffected: it emits `session:removed` + rebuilds under the same id, no `stopped`); this also fixes dead windows that kept polling a deleted session
- **Implementation**: new `task_config_queues` table (registered in the task schema) + new `task/config_queue.rs` module (outside the broadcast-pin scan scope; desktop UI refreshes via a `task:config-queue-changed` emit, session mode changes reuse the triple broadcast); commands `create-and-enqueue` (reworked) / `config-queues-list` / `config-queue-start` / `config-queue-set-auto` / `preset-enqueue-queue`; broadcast pins stay at baseline (bus/emit pair 9, ws outlet queue.rs 1)
- **Verified**: plugin crate `cargo test` 435 green; terminal-session app vitest 21 files / 244 tests green (H4 enqueue-without-create / H5 start & auto mode / H6 preset enqueue, H1 six-domain load); eslint 0 errors; vue-tsc only pre-existing errors (`item.job` / TerminalPreview / is_supported / test-mock types, all outside this diff or baseline)

#### Mobile terminal: kill the vertical black band on the right of TUIs, fix squeezed CJK spacing; fullscreen code viewer no longer invades the status bar

- **The black band on the right of TUIs (opencode) is gone**: grid sizing no longer reserves right-edge width (previously "6px scrollbar gutter + 1 column line-end margin"). The measured leftover shrank from **17px (4.2% of screen width)** to the unavoidable sub-cell remainder. Plain shell sessions (pi) paint everything with the terminal background so the defect was invisible there; a TUI paints its own background across its own column count, which turned the leftover into a black band. `TERMINAL_RIGHT_RESERVE_PX = 0` is the single source of truth (`computeGridSize` / `getXtermScaledDimensions` / `overviewRuler.width` all read it); line-end ink overflow is still absorbed by the constant `clip-path` on `.xterm-screen` (right-expanded 6px), and the custom scroll indicator now overlays the last column
- **Root cause of "CJK characters almost touching" fixed**: a real-device CDP probe showed `rows { letter-spacing: -1.5px }` = cell 7.5 − fallback 9.0. The bundled Sarasa font is declared `font-display: swap`, so `document.fonts.load()` resolved and `check()` was true while layout was **still on the system monospace font**; xterm's `WidthCache` cached that fallback width and never invalidated it, and DomRenderer then applied the difference as a per-character "row width compensation" — latin was squeezed by 1.5px and CJK (2 cells wide) by the same amount, so hanzi overlapped each other and drifted off the line end. Two fixes: (1) `ensureTerminalFontLoaded` now also **verifies layout** (frame-by-frame comparison of the single-character advance between the terminal font stack and a pure fallback stack); (2) right after `open()` the app assigns a "same font, different string" font family via `bustFontFamilyCache()`, forcing xterm to re-measure the cell width, clear the width cache and recompute the compensation (which lands at 0)
- **Fullscreen code viewer no longer invades the status bar**: in fullscreen the overlay drops `padding: 1rem` and the panel becomes truly fullscreen, with the safe area moved into the panel's own padding. Previously the panel's top edge sat at 16px while the status bar is 40px tall — the top 24px overlapped the status bar icon area
- **Verified**: mobile vitest 54 files / 535 tests green (new `terminalMetrics.test.ts` with 9 cases, `terminalDimensions.test.ts` updated); root eslint 0 errors (117 warnings = baseline); real-device CDP re-check of cell width / container width / letter-spacing

#### Agent Hub chat log: tool output cap 400 → 1000 chars, and pi's per-turn empty messages no longer render as zero-token bubbles

- **Tool output cap raised 400 → 1000** across all four adapters (`CODEX_TOOL_TEXT_CAP`, `OPENCODE_TOOL_TEXT_CAP`, plus claude / pi `tool_result` literals) — 400 chars was not enough to read a command output even after expanding the card. The display-layer mirror (`GUEST_TEXT_CAPS.toolOutput`) moves with it, and the tool-card collapse threshold goes 200 → 400 (40% of the cap) so short commands still show inline while long ones start collapsed
- **The display layer never character-truncates**: the DOM always holds the full guest text; the threshold only decides whether an expand control is offered
- **pi's per-turn empty assistant messages no longer become bubbles**: pi writes one `content: []` assistant message per turn with an empty `usage` object; the guest treats "usage is an object" as "has usage", so those rows landed as `↑0 ↓0 ⚡0 +0` bubbles — measured at **58 of 83** assistant messages in one real session. Assistant rows with no body and all-zero tokens are now dropped entirely, and all-zero token lines are never rendered (a row of zeros is not information); rows with real tokens but no body are kept so reasoning-only turns stay visible
- **Registered as a parser-side follow-up** (A10 in `.scratch/2026-10-03-agent-hub-chat-recon-gaps/README.md`): the guest may skip pushing such events entirely; the display-side guard already covers the wire as it stands
- **Verified**: agent-hub suite 432 tests green; plugin `cargo test --lib` 162 green; eslint 0 errors

#### Agent Hub chat log: markdown rendering, collapsible tool cards, truncated-content path

- **Markdown rendering for assistant replies** (was: raw plain text). New `src/utils/markdown.ts` is a **zero-dependency, escape-first CommonMark subset** renderer: paragraphs (soft line breaks → `br`), ATX headings, ordered / unordered lists (incl. two levels of indent nesting), blockquotes, thematic breaks, fenced code blocks (``` and ~~~), GFM pipe tables, and inline `code` / bold / italic / strikethrough / links. It escapes first and emits only its own whitelist of tags, so **no sanitizer is needed**; links intentionally render as inert text with the URL in a `title` tooltip (the host has no shell/opener permission and CSP `connect-src 'none'` — a clickable link would be a dead link that lies), and images degrade to their alt text (frontend zero-resource-access red line). Only assistant replies are rendered as markdown — user lines are "what I said" and tool lines are raw command output, neither should be typeset
- **Collapsed messages skip HTML generation**: the collapsed preview is marker-stripped plain text (`markdownPlainPreview`), because CSS line-clamp is only reliable on plain text; the structured HTML is produced on expand. Long messages therefore get the same 3-line + ellipsis affordance as before and cost one parse less
- **Why not marked** (resolves the open triage question): agent-hub is a **read-only log viewer** (no streaming, no composer), so pulling a parser + sanitizer in for a record-reading view is not worth it; both ai-chatbox ends already bundle marked + DOMPurify, but both sit on the streaming chat path
- **Tool rows became cards**: each tool line is split into a card header (type · name) and a card body (arguments / output) across all four adapter shapes (claude `tool_use` / `tool_result`, codex / opencode `tool · name (status) · body`, pi `name (error) · body`); unknown shapes fall back to header-less so output is never shredded. Bodies over 200 chars start **collapsed** (same pattern as the assistant collapse, with its own "Expand details" label), `tool_use · Bash` with no body renders no empty body block, and the trailing `(error)` that pi / opencode emit drives a danger left bar + semantic-danger card header
- **Truncated content now has a visible full-detail path**: the guest only appends an ellipsis when it really truncates, so the display layer spots that (trailing ellipsis + the **last ` · ` segment** longer than the smallest guest cap, 120) — per-segment rather than whole-text because a card can carry nested caps (claude's new 120-char argument summary makes `tool_use · name · args…` only ~140 chars in total, which a 400-char whole-text check would miss) and shows a note plus a "View raw JSONL" button that jumps to the raw tab — ui-ux-pro-max ux-guidelines "Essential Text Truncation (Critical): provide a visible full-detail path"
- **Still on the parser side** (unchanged here): call↔result pairing, `is_error` for claude / codex and tool argument summaries need the structured guest fields (`NormalizedEventView.error` / `toolUseId` are landing separately); the text-level `(error)` heuristic is the compatible fallback
- **Verified**: agent-hub suite 428 tests (49 new: 14 markdown behaviour contracts incl. XSS / `javascript:` URL / attribute-injection / placeholder-collision regressions, 14 tool-card + truncation contracts, 19 component-level) + full desktop vitest 1482 tests green; eslint 0 errors; agent-hub `tsc --noEmit` clean for the changed files


#### Agent Hub session logs: collapsible model output (collapsed by default) + JSONL parsing gap audit

- **Model output collapse (collapsed by default)**: in the chat-log view, assistant replies longer than 500 chars gain an "Expand / Collapse" control and start **collapsed** (3-line clamp + ellipsis preview) so long responses no longer flood the viewport. Short messages stay fully visible with no button (clamping cannot cut them, so the button would be noise); expansion state is tracked per event index and reset when switching sessions; the button carries `aria-expanded` / `aria-controls`. Design basis: ui-ux-pro-max ux-guidelines "Truncate with ellipsis and expand option"
- **JSONL→conversation reconstruction audit** (vs zcode / qoder and similar mature desktop GUI agents): confirmed information still being dropped —
  1. **pi adapter drops `toolCall` blocks entirely (real bug)**: the assistant preview reuses claude's `assistant_display_text`, which only matches claude's `tool_use` spelling; pi's actual content block type is `toolCall` (72 blocks in one real session), so tool calls never appear in pi assistant messages
  2. **`thinking` blocks are dropped by both adapters** (46 in one real pi session), so reasoning is invisible
  3. **Tool arguments are not shown**, only the tool name; `tool_result` `is_error` is not surfaced; `tool_use_id` is not linked to its `tool_result`
  4. **claude `attachment` lines are not parsed as attachment entries** (115 in one real session); `ai-title` lines are unused for better titles; image blocks inside `tool_result` are silently discarded
  5. Asymmetric output guards: opencode clamps oversized payloads (600/400), while **claude user messages are unbounded** into the event stream (serialization risk across the WASM boundary)
  6. Rendering gap vs mature GUIs: plain-text rendering (no markdown / code highlighting), tools have no standalone collapsible cards, failed tool results carry no visual marker
- **Scope of this change**: only the model-output collapse is implemented; the parser gaps (A1–A8) and display gaps (B1–B4) are fully registered in `.scratch/2026-10-03-agent-hub-chat-recon-gaps/README.md` (with on-machine evidence, fix suggestions, priorities and triage status) for separate, prioritized follow-ups
- **Parser fixes, first batch (info-loss level, all landed 2026-10-03)**: the audit's P0/P1 parsing gaps are fixed with fixtures —
  1. **pi `toolCall` blocks are no longer dropped** (was a real bug: 72 blocks in one real session): `assistant_display_text` now matches both claude's `tool_use` and pi's `toolCall`, rendering `tool_use · name · args-summary` (compact JSON, ≤120 chars)
  2. **Tool arguments are now shown**: claude `tool_use.input` gets the same compact-JSON summary instead of name-only (353 real blocks)
  3. **`tool_result` carries `is_error`** as a structured `error` flag on tool events (claude `is_error` / pi `isError`), wired through the event shape; **`tool_use_id` is now kept** (`toolUseId` in the wire) and claude results are **paired to their tool_use by id** (sequential stack) so the result line shows the tool name; **non-text blocks (images/binary) get a `[type 块，原始 JSONL 可查]` placeholder** instead of being silently filtered
  4. **claude `attachment` lines become system entries** (115 in one real session): `attachment · type · filename/prompt summary`, kept out of titles and token aggregation
  5. **claude (and pi, symmetric) user text is truncated to 2000 chars** before entering the event stream, matching the assistant guard (opencode was already guarded) — title still takes the first 120 of the untruncated text
- **Wire contract**: `NormalizedEvent` gains `error: bool` + `tool_use_id: Option<String>` (wire `error` / `toolUseId`), backward-compatible additive fields the display layer (B3) can consume for error styling and call↔result pairing
- **Verified**: agent-hub Rust suite 162 tests (11 new fixtures for A1/A3/A4/A5/A7, mutation-checked) + agent-hub frontend 423 + full desktop vitest 1482 green; eslint 0 errors on changed files; wasm release build + wasmHash injection pass; vue-tsc shows only the 2 pre-existing out-of-scope errors (StatsTab named exports)

#### Frontend zero-resource-access red line: no HTTP / WebSocket / file requests that bypass the permission gate (three enforcement layers)

- **Principle**: the frontend is UI display only and carries no backend logic. HTTP / WebSocket / file access is always initiated on the Rust side and goes through permission arbitration (`host-*` primitives + egress / approval gates) — a direct frontend call bypasses those gates
- **Audit**: both frontends already had **zero** `fetch` / `XMLHttpRequest` / `WebSocket` (the only literal hits are inside a markdown code-block **string** in ai-chatbox `dev-mock.ts`, never executed); resource access was already funnelled into `invoke(...)` → host commands / plugin command surfaces. The exposure was not existing code but the **absence of a lock** — the next `fetch` could be written straight in
- **Three layers**, outermost first — layers 2 and 3 cannot be worked around by changing frontend code:
  1. **Source convention** — root `eslint.config.js` gains `bedcode/frontend-no-resource-access`, one config covering both ends (host `src/` + business wasm app / mobile plugin `src/` + both SDK frontends), enforced by the CI `lint.yml` 0-error gate: bans `fetch` / `XMLHttpRequest` / `WebSocket` / `EventSource` / `navigator.sendBeacon` (including `window.fetch` / `globalThis['fetch']` spellings), imports of network- or file-capable Tauri plugins, and the global `__TAURI__` handle (both ends set `withGlobalTauri: true`, so calls through it slip past import-level checks)
  2. **Capability layer (enforced by Rust at runtime)** — `shell:allow-open` and `updater:default` are removed from both ends' `capabilities/*.json`. Tauri checks `plugin:*` commands against the ACL unconditionally: a permission absent from capabilities yields `acl.is_none()` and a runtime reject (`tauri/src/webview/mod.rs`), so the frontend cannot reach it no matter how it writes the call. The desktop additionally drops the `tauri-plugin-shell` dependency, removing one "just add a capability" way back
  3. **Engine layer (enforced by the browser)** — both ends' `tauri.conf.json` `app.security.csp` goes from `null` to `{ "connect-src": "'none'" }`, sealing off `fetch` / `XMLHttpRequest` / `WebSocket` / `EventSource` / `sendBeacon` regardless of JS spelling and independent of Tauri. IPC travels over `postMessage` and plugin frontend bundles load via `import()` (governed by `script-src`), so neither is affected by `connect-src`; dev-mode HMR is separately allowed through the new `devCsp`
- **Initiation moves to Rust**: the frontend no longer holds the updater or OS-open call surfaces
  - `check_for_update` / `install_update`: the update check and install are initiated by host commands (Rust-side egress + minisign pubkey verification); the frontend only receives already-verified version metadata and `app://update-progress` events. `install_update` returns a `bool` meaning "did installation start" so no branch relies on error-message matching
  - `open_external_url`: the About section's project link goes through a host command gated by `system::opener::validate_external_url`, an http/https scheme allowlist that is fail-closed (rejects empty strings, missing `:`, illegal scheme tokens, `file:` / `javascript:` / `data:` / `smb:` and friends, and any control characters). The desktop switched to `tauri-plugin-opener` because `Shell::open` is deprecated, matching the mobile `open_url_in_browser` path
- **Anti-regression lock**: new `bedcode-desktop/src-tauri/src/capabilities_test.rs` (7 tests) pins layers 2 and 3 — forbidden permission prefixes in both ends' capabilities, no `tauri-plugin-{http,fs,shell}` dependency, **revoking a capability and adding its host command must ship together** (so a revoke-only change fails loudly instead of silently breaking a feature), and both ends' CSP must contain `connect-src 'none'` and must not be reset to `null`. The lock carries positive self-checks so the extractor must fail on a real regression rather than staying green
- **Verified**: an ESLint probe covering 18 spellings is blocked in full and then deleted; repo-wide lint reports 0 errors / 117 warnings (unchanged from baseline); all 7 lock tests pass; 9 opener allowlist unit tests (positive cases, scheme negatives, lexical boundaries, control-character smuggling, lexical-vs-allowlist branch attribution); 17 frontend update-checker tests pass, with four new contracts (subscription established before invoke, cumulative byte writes, "no update available" is not a failure, unlisten on every exit path)
- **Correction**: this work also showed that my earlier claim — "`shell:allow-open` fails closed without a scope, so the button most likely did nothing" — was **wrong**. With the config `Unset`, `tauri-plugin-shell` installs a built-in default scope regex `^((mailto:\w+)|(tel:\w+)|(https?://\w+)).+` that matches the GitHub URL, so the button was genuinely working. That is exactly why revoking it is a real lockdown rather than a no-op
- **Not done / open**: the mobile production `connect-src 'none'` was only checked statically (the mobile frontend likewise makes zero network calls) and has **not been exercised on a real device** — Android needs on-device verification. Desktop dev mode serves pages from the vite dev server (bypassing the Tauri custom protocol, so no CSP is injected), meaning the real interception effect should be judged on a release build

#### Release builds now suppress the native right-click menu in every window, not only the main one

- **Symptom**: on Linux the release build still popped WebKitGTK’s built-in right-click menu (Inspect / Reload / Back) inside session terminal windows. The suppression was wired once during startup to the `main` window only; Windows/macOS were already covered by the frontend `preventDefault`, which runs in every window because they all load the same frontend bundle
- **What changed**: a new `native_context_menu_guard` plugin connects the WebKitGTK `context-menu` signal from a page-load hook, which fires for **every** webview — the main window and each session terminal window created at runtime. Release builds only, and each webview is connected once (every navigation re-fires the page-load hook, and `connect_context_menu` appends a handler, so repeated connects would stack them); dev builds keep the right-click menu for debugging
- **Design note**: the dev/release switch is a `cfg!(debug_assertions)` early return inside the hook rather than a `#[cfg(...)]` around the whole block, so the release path is literally the code that debug builds compile and unit-test — a release-only path should not be a black box that only gets compiled at ship time
- **Verified**: desktop `cargo test` **888 lib tests + every integration target green** (4 new unit tests: per-webview connect-once, cross-webview independence, dedupe key correctness, poisoned-lock resilience). Mutation probes: dropping the dedupe turns 3 of them red, dropping the poison recovery turns the 4th red

#### Auto-task state machine gains a “retrying” status: tasks are no longer marked interrupted while the agent is auto-retrying

- **Semantics (aligned with the host task definition)**: a task is only marked interrupted when the agent execution has fully stopped and the task is incomplete. While the agent auto-retries a retryable error (e.g. 429), the task stays active and shows “Retrying”; success converges to completed, retry exhaustion converges to interrupted — never a terminal state mid-retry
- **Pi adapter (pi_task_hook.ts)**: pushes `retrying` when `after_provider_response` returns a retryable HTTP status (429 / 408 / 5xx) or `agent_end` carries a retryable error message; the terminal decision stays at `agent_settled` (fires once after all automatic retries/compaction/queued work) — completed on success, interrupted on exhaustion
- **Claude Code adapter (auto_task_hook.py)**: new `StopFailure` hook event — Claude retries transient errors (429/5xx) internally with exponential backoff (up to 10×, invisible to hooks), during which the task stays `in_progress`; when retries are exhausted and the turn ends due to an API error (`StopFailure` is mutually exclusive with `Stop`), the task converges to `interrupted` (previously it could hang in `in_progress` forever or be mislabeled completed)
- **opencode adapter (opencode_task_hook.ts)**: `session.status(retry)`, `session.next.step.failed`, and non-user-abort `session.error` all push `retrying`
- **Host state machine (terminal-session plugin)**: `task-status` whitelist gains `retrying`; session-end fallback interrupt now covers `retrying` rows; idle pushes no longer downgrade `retrying` rows
- **Display**: desktop task history/queue, mobile session cards and the task-history tab all render “Retrying” with an amber status color; i18n synced in both locales
- **Not covered (agent capability boundary, not fixable here)**: Claude Code exposes no hook event for its internal retries or API transport errors (Anthropic feature requests #46959/#70026); Codex likewise has no API-error hook signal (openai/codex #22774)

#### Auto-task context isolation now rotates sessions (close the completed session, create a fresh one with the same config) instead of sending clear/new commands

- **What changed**: between tasks in an auto-task queue the plugin no longer sends `/clear` (claude/codex) or `/new` (pi) into the terminal to rebuild context. It now closes the completed session, creates a **fresh session from the same config** (configId), migrates the remaining queue to it, and dispatches the next task once the new session is ready (agent SessionStart idle). Each task runs in a clean context — the overall effect matches the old clear-based flow
- **Session-id bookkeeping follows the move (the rotation function is the single migration point)**: `task_queue` waiting + pending rows are re-keyed to the new session; `task_session_settings` (auto_execute / auto_answer) are copied to the new session; `task_scheduled` executed archives migrate their session_id (so the final session is still auto-closed when the queue drains); `task_history` / `task_session_mapping` stay under the old session (history and bookkeeping)
- **Narrower readiness windows + grace fallback (no more cancel)**: waiting for the new session now uses 3s / 5s / 8s per attempt (timer resets each retry, ~16s total). When idle still has not arrived after the third window the task is **dispatched directly** (replacing the old "cancel"): it covers agents without an idle signal and hooks going silent — a failed input is settled by the dispatch fallback instead of the task silently disappearing
- **opencode rotates too** (it has no /clear and its agent session is only created by the first submitted prompt): its fresh session has no idle signal, so readiness is settled by the grace dispatch (the ~16s grace covers its measured ~9s TUI boot from PTY start to input ready) — the session is synchronously created during rotation, and the grace guarantees the ordering "input only after the session has been created successfully"
- **Verified**: terminal-session plugin native tests **429 green** (rotation decision now covers opencode / window tests); host closed-loop `test_session_task_rotation_closed_loop` drives two real-PTY rotations (sid1 → sid2 → sid3) plus the grace-dispatch scenario, asserting queue migration, flag copying, old-session Stopped convergence, idle-driven dispatch, grace dispatch without the empty-queue guard closing the session

#### Mobile terminal input is fixed: a command + Enter sent one Enter keypress and silently dropped the command text

- **The symptom this fixes**: on the mobile terminal page nothing typed ever reached the desktop shell — the ▶ button, the keyboard's enter, and every quick-bar command (`/model`, `/skill:`, …) appeared to do nothing, with no error, no toast, and nothing in the log. Shortcut *keys* (Enter/Del/arrows/Ctrl+C) also looked dead, because the Enter key only submitted an empty prompt
- **Root cause**: the mobile WS terminal link's `terminal_send_input` command built its frames with mutually exclusive branches — `if a special key is present { send only the key bytes } else if data is non-empty { send the text }`. The input bar's execute path **always** passes `specialKey: "enter"` together with the command text, so every send reduced to a bare `\r` and the text was discarded. The function's own doc comment already specified the opposite ("both forms may coexist, frame order is write order") — the code contradicted its stated contract
- **Proof of the defect, not of a guess**: driving the real command from the page sent the text frame alone and the text appeared at the prompt (`| ZZ_PLAIN`), while sending text + Enter produced an *empty* submitted line. The Rust → WS → plugin → PTY path itself was healthy (a direct invoke with the same payload executed `echo RUST_PROBE_MARKER` on the desktop)
- **What changed**: the frame plan is now built by a pure, unit-tested function `plan_input_frames` — text first, key bytes second, in write order. An unsupported key name is now **validated before anything is sent** (no half-written input), and the empty key name `Some("")` is treated as "no key"
- **Coverage gap that let it ship** (answered, with the hole now filled): `cross-end-tests`' `terminal_ws_flow` C-004 exercised the **HTTP** `session-input` face, while the mobile input bar and quick-bar commands actually use the **WS** face. The two never met in a test, and the HTTP face was green the whole time. Two new contracts drive the real WS face against a real PTY: C-005 (text + Enter must actually execute the command) and C-006 (a `ctrl+u` key frame must kill the line, proven together with a positive control so "every frame lost" cannot pass)
- **Verified**: mobile `cargo test` **344 green / 0 red** (7 new unit tests: text-only, key-only, text+key ordering, order observability, unknown key rejected without partial send, empty key name, empty both). Mutation probes: restoring the old exclusive branch turns 2 unit tests and cross-end C-005 red; dropping only the `ctrl+u` frame turns C-006 red (`left: 1, right: 0`). `cross-end-tests` `terminal_ws_flow` green; mobile vitest 53 files / 524 tests green; root `eslint .` 0 errors / 117 warnings

#### Mobile scrollbar gutter is now opt-in per container instead of a blanket `*` rule — the terminal no longer loses 69px of width to nine nested wrappers

- **The symptom this fixes**: the terminal page showed a white ~32px gutter down its right edge and the terminal grid was visibly narrower than the screen. Measured on-device: viewport 711px, `.xterm-screen` 642px — **69px (10%) of the width simply vanished**
- **Root cause**: the anti-CLS fix from `46e416618` added `* { scrollbar-gutter: stable }`. On this WebView `::-webkit-scrollbar` is space-consuming (8px, not overlay), so the rule reserved a gutter on **every `overflow:hidden` element**. The terminal page stacks nine of them (`.terminal-view` → `.movable-clip` → `.movable-area` → `.main-content` → `.terminal-output-area` → `.terminal-scroll-container` → `.xterm-container` → `.xterm` → `.xterm-scrollable-element`), costing 8px each; the exposed `.terminal-view` background (`#faf9f5`) is what the user saw as the gutter. That commit had already exempted two wrappers for exactly this reason — the terminal chain was simply missed
- **What changed**: the blanket rule is gone. The single utility class `.scrollbar-gutter-stable` now carries the rule, and it is applied only to the containers that actually scroll page content (the `flex-1` content scrollers of the views and `SettingsSubPage`), preserving the original CLS protection where it was needed. Deliberately **not** applied to: `overflow:hidden` layout wrappers (never show a scrollbar — pure dead space) and fixed-width overlays such as modals, input-bar completion popups, and the file sidebar (a permanent 8px gutter would squeeze their content)
- **Verified on-device**: with the equivalent rule live-injected into the running app, `.terminal-view` / `.movable-area` / `.terminal-output-area` / `.xterm` / `.xterm-scrollable-element` all measure the full 711px and `.xterm-screen` grows 642 → 696px; the screenshot shows the gutter gone and the quick-bar/input bar spanning the full width. Mobile vitest 53 files / 524 tests green; root `eslint .` 0 errors / 117 warnings

#### Agent Hub "check for updates" now re-detects the installed CLI versions first, so the outdated badge and the shown local version match reality (desktop agent-hub plugin only; no ABI/WIT/protocol change)

- **The symptom this fixes**: with pi already at 1.0.0, the Install tab's "Check for updates" still showed `0.87 → 1.0` as outdated; only going back to the Overview tab and running "Re-detect" made the row show 1.0. The update check compared the registry `latest` against the **cached** version from the *last* full detect, and — unlike Overview's re-detect — never re-ran the local `--version` probes, so any install/update done outside the hub stayed invisible until a full re-detect refreshed the cache
- **What changed**: `agent-hub.check-updates` no longer reads the stale detect cache synchronously. It sets a pending flag and triggers the same full re-detect that Overview uses (`spawn_all`); when the whole batch of probes converges (`is_batch_done`: the run is removed from the batch set **and** the set becomes empty — a stale run from an older batch, whose id is not in the set, can never finish the batch), the plugin automatically runs the registry `latest` query against the **fresh** local versions and emits the install state. The UI flow is unchanged (the rows briefly show "detecting", then the real version and a correct outdated badge)
- **Concurrency and cleanup**: the batch set is replaced wholesale on each `spawn_all`, so a second click mid-detect just starts a newer batch and the stale completions are ignored (remove-miss). The pending flag is cleared when the batch converges, when the batch fails to spawn a single run, when `spawn_all` itself errors, and in `deactivate` — a lingering flag cannot fire an update check out of the blue on a later plain detect
- **Verified**: agent-hub crate `cargo test` **153 green / 0 red** (5 new unit tests: batch-done on last run, stale run never finishes a batch, outdated=true when local behind, false when local equal/newer, null when local unknown); `wasm32-wasip3` release build green with wasmHash re-injected; desktop `system_component_test` (loads the real agent-hub artifact) 10 green. Mutation probes: dropping the `is_empty` check turns `batch_done_on_last_run` red; flipping the outdated comparison turns `outdated_flag_marks_local_behind` red; returning `Some` for a missing local version turns `outdated_flag_unknown_local_is_null` red
- **Not changed**: the post-install auto re-detect (already in `install::handle_process_done`) keeps its current semantics — it refreshes local versions but does **not** auto-query latest; the user can hit "Check for updates" to get the badge. Pre-existing `cargo fmt` debt in `providers/apply.rs` / `usage/scan.rs` and the `vec!`/collapsible-`if` clippy notes in untouched files are left alone

#### Link-encryption negotiation is now fail-visible: an encrypted request arriving at a desktop that has encryption **off** is rejected with an explicit 4xx instead of being fed to the business layer

- **The symptom this fixes**: with encryption enabled on the mobile but **not** on the desktop, a POST with a body returned `HTTP 200` and `{"code":1002,"message":"configId required"}` — the ciphertext was passed straight through to the plugin, which parsed it as JSON and reported a misleading *business* error. GET was worse in a quieter way: there is no request body to decrypt and the response carries no encryption marker, so the mobile (strict mode off by default) silently continued in plaintext. A 200 plus "missing parameter" sends the user hunting for a config problem that does not exist
- **Root cause — the original design spec contradicts itself**: `.scratch/http-ws-payload-encryption/spec.md` §6 decision rule 3 ("response binding") says that once a request carries the negotiation header the peer **must** participate regardless of its own current switch, and §6 rule 2 says the receiver identifies participation from the wire signal. But the same section's implementation paragraph says the filter is **not registered** when the master switch is off, and §7's compatibility matrix calls "either side off" simply plaintext. "Not registered" means nothing on the chain ever reads the negotiation header, so "identify from the signal" cannot happen. The implementation took the "not registered" branch
- **What changed (option B, chosen deliberately)**: the opt-in baseline is untouched — encryption still defaults to off, the filter is still not registered when the switch is off, and plaintext traffic is still unaffected. Only the **silent** part is gone: the HTTP wiring point now reads the negotiation header before its fast path and, when link-crypto is not on the chain, answers `400` with the same `CODE_INVALID_REQUEST` envelope used by filter rejections plus a message that **names the switch** (`trafficEncryption`) and both ends' remedies. GET is rejected the same way, so "GET silently passes, POST mysteriously fails" cannot happen
- **Deliberately honest about a tightening**: in that mismatch state POST used to "succeed" with a garbage business code and GET used to pass in plaintext; both now fail visibly. Unreadable ciphertext passing silently costs more than an explicit refusal. The mobile's `strictMode` downgrade notice does not fire on this path (the response never reaches the decryption step) — the user gets an actionable 400 instead
- **Not changed**: the loopback exemption keeps its hard constraint (local calls are never encrypted), so a loopback peer sending a negotiation header still degrades the old way — there is no realistic producer of that (neither the desktop WebView nor hook scripts go through the mobile proxy). `HEAD` and `/ws` are out of scope for the same reason (no body to encrypt; WS frame encryption is retired). Option A (change the semantics so a desktop with the switch off still participates on seeing the header) was **not** adopted — it would overturn the "off means zero overhead" opt-in baseline; if it is ever wanted, the §6/§7 contradiction should first be settled in an ADR
- **Verified**: `bedcode-server-http` 53+4 green (3 new unit tests: rejected when unregistered / not caught when the filter *is* registered / default-state plaintext not caught), `bedcode-server-core` 41 green, desktop `cargo test` 884 lib green / 0 red plus all integration targets, `cross-end-tests` 11/11 green with a new contract (P-006) pinning the rejection shape end to end. A mutation probe disabling the new branch turns P-006 red **and reproduces the original symptom verbatim** (`got 200 body={"code":1002,"message":"configId required"}`), so the assertion really pins the old behaviour. Both crates `cargo fmt --check` clean; root `eslint .` 0 errors / 117 warnings
- **Where the contradiction is recorded**: `.scratch/2026-10-02-encryption-negotiation-fail-visible.md` (the behaviour is fail-visible now; the spec contradiction itself is still only annotated in code, not yet settled in an ADR)

#### Cross-end coverage extension: event channel, HTTP proxy + link-crypto envelope, mDNS + health (3 new test binaries; one additive mobile API; no ABI, no protocol, no version move)

- **What**: the 2026-09-30 cross-end suite covered authentication → session HTTP → terminal stream, but three production links on the mobile side had **zero** real-interop coverage: the `session-control` event channel (all seven event frames), the `http_request` proxy that **every** mobile HTTP call goes through (Egress gate + JWT injection + link-crypto envelope), and mDNS discovery + `/api/health` (how a user finds a desktop in the first place). Three new scenario binaries, each its own process like the existing eight
- **Event channel** (`event_channel_flow.rs`): the seven event frames really round-trip between the desktop plugin's broadcast and the mobile's event router — session lifecycle (`session:created/stopped/removed`), task/mode/scheduled events with per-field assertions. The load-bearing contracts are the negative ones: a **forged JWT** connection is closed with an auth-fatal code and receives **zero** event frames; an **unexpected drop** self-heals into a *new* connection (asserted by client id, not by an empty window); an **auth-fatal** close does **not** self-heal, and after re-authentication the missed events are **not replayed** — the gap is closed by the HTTP reconcile instead
- **HTTP proxy** (`http_proxy_flow.rs`): Egress L1 rejects undeclared targets, `https://` escapes, and consent-required URLs when there is no UI to ask (fail-closed); JWT injection is pinned in both directions (no token → 401 with business code 1007, forged token → 401, real token → 200); the **link-crypto envelope** is proven in both directions by the desktop's own encryption counters — a POST with a body moves `encrypted_frames` by +2 with zero decrypt failures, a GET moves it for the response, and the `/api/auth/*` whitelist moves it by **+0** (so "encrypt everything" cannot pass). The `resize` endpoint — which has **no Rust client on the mobile side** — is exercised here for the first time
- **mDNS + health** (`mdns_health_flow.rs`): the desktop advertises and the mobile really browses `_bedcode._tcp.local.`, then asserts the discovered port, `platform`/`device_name`/`version` TXT records, address and hostname field by field; the health probe asserts `{status, port, uptime_secs}` with the port matching the discovered one
- **One additive mobile API** (the only production change): `MdnsDiscovery::start` takes an `AppHandle`, whose runtime type is `Wry` — a headless process cannot construct one. It is split into `start` / `start_headless` / `start_inner(Option<AppHandle>)`; browsing, parsing and caching are **shared verbatim**, and `app_handle = None` only skips the front-end `emit` (which was already `let _ =`). Behaviour is unchanged; the mobile remains a self-contained app, so the host "no business code" red line does not apply
- **Honest boundaries (documented, not hidden)**: the desktop's link-crypto filter **exempts loopback peers** by design (hook scripts and local tools stay plaintext), so the crypto scenario connects via the machine's own LAN address — a rig connecting to `127.0.0.1` would silently skip the encrypted branch and pass as "plaintext 200". `/api/health` reports the port the **supervisor** owns, so the mDNS scenario must start the server through the supervisor (the production path); the light `start_server` entry bypasses it and leaves health reporting the default port 8765. The `version` TXT record's source of truth is `SystemInfoPort::app_version()` (2.1.1), **not** `SystemInfo::collect().app_version` (which is the `bedcode-server-base` *package* version, 0.1.0 — a naming trap recorded, not changed). `SyncConfig*` has no frame source on this channel, so it is not covered. peer-net P2P file transfer, the biometric positive path and the QR scan UI stay out of scope
- **Verified**: `cross-end-tests` **11/11 green**, twice in a row; the three new binaries each re-run 3-4 times for stability. Mutation probes: breaking the AAD path binding on the mobile side → desktop `AES-256-GCM` failure → 400 (so route binding is genuinely enforced across the wire); dropping JWT injection → 401 instead of 200; letting the supervisor self-heal an auth-fatal close → the "no events may land during an outage" assertion goes red; a wrong `platform` TXT → red. All probes reverted verbatim (`git diff` on both ends' production sources is empty). Regression: desktop `cargo test` **884 lib green / 0 red** + all integration targets; mobile `cargo test` **337 green / 0 red**; mobile vitest **53 files / 524 tests**; desktop vitest **112 files / 1422 tests**; root `eslint .` **0 errors / 117 warnings** (unchanged); cross-end `clippy --tests` **0 diagnostics** in this package. Not run: `pnpm run tauri:build`, real-device checks, Kotlin compilation (no UI or Kotlin changes)
- **Test-infrastructure note**: the mDNS scenario's failure path used to cost ~120s because unwinding dropped a running `mdns_sd::ServiceDaemon` (joining its receive thread) and left the actix runtime on a **non-daemon** OS thread alive; teardown now runs via `catch_unwind` + resume, so a failing assertion costs 1.0s

#### Mobile terminal: keyboard avoidance switched from resize to lift, plus a bundled strictly-monospaced CJK font (mobile frontend only; no protocol/ABI/version move)

- **What (keyboard avoidance)**: the old implementation was resize-based — the `terminal-view` root shrank to `height = calc(100vh - keyboardHeight)` → `ResizeObserver` re-fit → **rows were recomputed and a fresh PTY resize was sent on every keyboard show/hide**. It is now the same **lift** the shortcut panel already uses: `.movable-area` (terminal canvas + input bar) gets `translateY(-keyboardOffset)`, with the top clipped under the Header by `.movable-clip` (`overflow: hidden`). Grid cols/rows and the PTY size stay untouched ⇒ keyboard show/hide causes **zero reflow**, no full-screen TUI repaint, no buffer re-wrap. The old implementation is **kept as a comment** at the end of `useTerminalKeyboardAvoidance.ts` (with a three-step restore recipe), and a new unit test asserts `terminalViewStyle` carries no `height` so the resize semantics cannot silently return
- **Honest cost (not a bug)**: as many top rows are clipped as the keyboard is tall, so landscape + keyboard shows noticeably fewer rows. Lift is only correct while the layout viewport is not compressed by the keyboard (`AndroidManifest windowSoftInputMode=adjustNothing`); that precondition and its failure signature are documented in the composable header — on a device that honours `interactive-widget=resizes-content` the measured offset exceeds the real keyboard height, and the fix is to look at the ICB, not to add a fudge factor
- **Compositor cleanup**: both translate sources (shortcut panel and keyboard) leave stale frame tiles when restored (observed on device as off-white bands), so the existing "full refresh + forced re-composite after the animation" timer now watches both offsets
- **What (font)**: the terminal now bundles a **Sarasa Mono SC subset** (SIL OFL-1.1) — Latin 0.5em / CJK 1em (= 2 cells) / box-drawing 0.5em. The single reason is to **fix the right-edge raggedness and the TUI background blocks that spill past the grid**: the system monospace font gives the Latin advance (~0.6em) while CJK falls back to a proportional CJK font at 1em, so `1em ≠ 2×0.6em` and the sub-pixel error accumulates per character (exactly the mechanism recorded in `terminal.css` under "行尾软裁切"). Android and Chinese ROMs ship no CJK **monospace** font (Noto Sans Mono CJK is absent), so it has to be bundled
- **Not bundling the full font**: the complete `SarasaMonoSC-Regular.ttf` is 14MB, which is unacceptable in an APK. `scripts/build-terminal-font.mjs` (+ devDep `subset-font`) subsets it to what a terminal actually shows: the full GB2312 set (6763 hanzi) + box drawing + block elements + punctuation + math/arrow/technical symbols + kana + full/half-width forms = 10635 code points, producing a **1.05MB woff2**. Hanzi outside the subset (traditional, rare) fall back to a system CJK font whose advance is always 1em = 2×0.5em, so the "CJK = 2 cells" invariant — and therefore the alignment — still holds
- **Font-ready ordering (skip it and the grid is wrong)**: the cell width changes from the fallback's ~0.6em to the bundled 0.5em, so measuring too early makes one screen compute cols/rows under two different metrics (right-edge misalignment, fit thrash). `ensureTerminalFontLoaded()` (3s timeout, falls back and `logger.warn`s on failure) is awaited before the first measurement in `initTerminal` and before the device-budgeted starting grid in the sessions view; the `@font-face` declaration is global (`styles/terminal-font.css`) while the woff2 itself is still fetched on demand, so **startup is not slowed**
- **Licensing**: the licence text ships next to the font (`src/assets/fonts/LICENSE-Sarasa-Gothic.txt`, OFL clause 2) and the subset keeps every `name`-table record so the copyright notice travels inside the binary; the primary font name does not contain the reserved name `'Source'` declared by its CJK portion (OFL clause 3)
- **Verified**: 15 new unit tests (dual-channel detection + the 10px threshold + lift style + the anti-resize regression lock + hide callback + dispose unbinding); four manual mutation probes (`>`→`>=`, threshold gate, channel priority, baseline freezing) each turn the matching case red; mobile `vitest` **53 files / 524 tests green**, `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, root `eslint .` **0 errors**, `vite build` emits the hashed woff2 and the CSS references it correctly. **Not verified on a real device** (keyboard show/hide feel, TUI box-drawing joins, full-width CJK line ends) — needs `pnpm run tauri:android:dev` on Android hardware

#### Cross-end real-interop integration test suite (new `cross-end-tests` package + both-ends `lib` rename; no production logic, no ABI, no version move)

- **What**: a new top-level Rust package `cross-end-tests/` runs the **desktop's real Actix server + the real `com.bedcode.terminal-session` WASM artifact** in the same process as the **mobile's real client code** (`AuthHttpClient` / `SessionHttpClient` / `TerminalLinkManager`) — zero mocks, no adb, no WebView, no emulator, and **run on demand — deliberately not wired into a workflow** (decided 2026-09-30: the build prerequisite is both ends' plugin artifacts, and rebuilding them in CI costs more than it buys; command is `cd cross-end-tests && cargo test`, see AGENTS §3). The pre-existing tests each mock the *other* end (desktop integration uses a generic reqwest / tokio-tungstenite client; mobile integration uses a fake desktop server). Two self-consistent mocks mean a wire-contract drift between the ends ships green on both sides while the real link is broken
- **L0 prerequisite — both `lib` names de-duplicated**: `bedcode_lib` (identical in both packages) → `bedcode_desktop_lib` / `bedcode_mobile_lib`. Two crates with the same name cannot coexist in one dependency graph, so without this the package could only depend on one end. Impact audited and contained to the two ends' own sources/tests/benches plus a handful of string constants (EnvFilter default `bedcode_lib=debug`, the plugin-log target prefix, `System.loadLibrary` in the hand-kept `android-backup` Kotlin copy, and four doc tables). A path dependency keys on the *package* name by default, so `Cargo.toml` names `package = "..."` explicitly
- **Documented behaviour change, not silent**: an operator's existing `bedcode_lib=debug` EnvFilter (and any log rule filtering on the plugin-log target) must switch to the new names. These are developer-tuning settings, not product features. The Android `.so` filename follows the lib name, so `System.loadLibrary("bedcode_mobile_lib")` changes too; the `gen/android` copy is gitignored and regenerated, but the hand-kept `android-backup/app-java/generated/Rust.kt` copy was updated by hand so a restore still matches
- **Coverage (each scenario = its own test binary — `AppContext` is a process-level `OnceLock`)**: pairing / QR / re-auth round-trips with four negative cases; the ADR 0033 keyring **rotation grace period proven from the client side** (a pre-rotation token still authenticates on both the HTTP and the WS face, and post-rotation tokens carry the new `kid`); session-control HTTP surface incl. the `1002` error envelope and the deliberate *remove-idempotent vs stop/input-strict* asymmetry; the **terminal-stream closed loop** where real `bash` PTY bytes travel desktop-plugin → `host-pty` → WS → mobile link ingest gate → page channel; fail-closed for "no auth centre registered" and forged credentials on both the HTTP and WS faces; plugin deactivate/activate ripple onto the mobile connection
- **A cross-end-only finding**: two of the first-draft contracts were wrong, and the real behaviour is the more interesting one. (a) With no auth centre registered the pairing endpoint is **401, not 404** — the no-credential tier is declared by the plugin at activate time, so with no activation there is *no unauthenticated bootstrap path at all*. (b) `remove` on a missing session is **idempotent by design** (documented in `actions::remove_via_host`) while `stop`/`input` are strict — that asymmetry is now pinned cross-end. Both are recorded in the test contracts, not worked around
- **Honest boundaries (documented, not hidden)**: `deny_kind`'s three states are a host **log** field, not a wire field — the client only ever sees 401 (deliberate, to avoid leaking deployment state); the biometric positive path needs an Android Keystore private key that a headless process cannot produce (only the "not bound → 1008" negative is covered); the QR "scan-to-confirm" UI step is bypassed by driving the plugin's own `qr-code-generate` entry point
- **Not wired into CI**: no job was added to `test.yml` (decided 2026-09-30, run on demand). The cost and its mitigation: cross-end contract drift is no longer caught automatically at PR time; it rests on the manual gate "changing a cross-end protocol must run `cross-end-tests`" in AGENTS §10 plus the cross-reference from the protocol chapter in `docs/knowledge/mobile-desktop-auth.md`
- **Verified**: both-ends `cargo test` green after the rename (desktop lib 1058 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored plus all integration targets; mobile 379 passed / 0 failed); `cross-end-tests` 7/7 green; desktop vitest 1422, mobile vitest 509, root `eslint .` 0 errors; cross-end `cargo fmt --check` and `clippy --tests` clean; mobile `./gradlew :app:compileUniversalDebugKotlin` BUILD SUCCESSFUL; `cargo build --lib` emits `libbedcode_desktop_lib.so` (artifact name follows the lib name, measured); no leftover test processes or ports

> Mostly desktop-only work (roadmap stage 2 + stage 3-session part, executed as one merged
> batch), plus one mobile infrastructure change (wasmtime 47 → 48, see Platform &
> Infrastructure) — **no version-number moves on either end**; the recorded cross-platform
> exemption and the mobile impact list below apply to the desktop batches.

#### Test fixtures under `packages/` consolidate into one feature-selected crate (desktop test infrastructure; no production code, no ABI, no version move)

- **What**: the six SDK-bound fixtures `plugin-http-test` / `plugin-task-test` / `plugin-pty-test` / `plugin-wasip3-test` / `plugin-sdk-test` / `plugin-ws-test` collapse into a single `packages/plugin-sdk-fixtures` crate, one per `[features]` slot, and are **deleted**. `plugin-component-test` (hand-written wit-bindgen fixture, 30+ call sites) is **deleted too** — its four observable behaviours (`test.panic`, `test.storage-get`, startup-failure injection, and the `name`/`args`/`stored` reply shape) moved into the `sdk` fixture, and its DB round-trip moved to a new `test.db-roundtrip` command so that `test.echo` — used by the fuel and latency probes — does not carry two extra table round-trips. The six near-verbatim "mtime check + `cargo build`" blocks become one parameterised `build_sdk_fixture(feature)`; each existing builder is now a one-line delegation
- **Mutually-exclusive features are load-bearing, but not for the reason one would guess**: a spike disproved the obvious story. Two features compiled together do **not** collide — `wasm_entry!` / `export!` inside separate modules emit non-conflicting symbols, and the component is ~4x larger with both fixtures in. The real reason for one-feature-per-build is **artifact ambiguity**: `build_sdk_fixture` archives by feature name, so one artifact must map unambiguously to one fixture. Cargo cannot express "at most one", so `compile_error!` enforces it
- **Artifact-name collision and the race it caused**: all features emit the same `bedcode_plugin_sdk_fixtures.wasm`, so a later build overwrites an earlier one. Fixed by archiving each build to a feature-suffixed name. That alone was not enough — with tests running in parallel, one thread could archive *another* thread's half-written file (observed as `failed to parse WebAssembly module`), so build+archive is now serialised behind a process-wide mutex with a double-check of the freshness cache after acquiring it
- **A drift lock caught a real coverage hole this created, not just a count change**: `production_manifests_declare_only_known_vocabulary` asserts how many manifests it scanned, and dropped from 11 to 7 when five `plugin.json` files became one crate's per-feature `http.json` / `pty.json` / `task.json` / `ws.json` + root `plugin.json`. The lock only recognised the single-fixture `plugin.json` name, so **4 fixture manifests had silently fallen out of the permission-vocabulary check**. It now scans every `*.json` in each package/app dir and keeps only those carrying an `id` field (which skips `package.json` / `tsconfig.json`); the floor is raised to 11 with the composition spelled out
- **Cost accepted (deliberate)**: with `plugin-component-test` gone, nothing can produce a component that *omits* an optional interface — the SDK's `wasm_entry!` exports all of them unconditionally. The three degradation tests that relied on such an artifact are removed, so **"an old plugin artifact still loads, and a missing optional export degrades to `Ok(false)`" is no longer covered**. The host's probe-and-degrade code is unchanged; only the test coverage of it is gone
- **`plugin-wasi-test` stays a separate crate and stays on `wasm32-wasip2`**: preopen was migrated to wasip3 on the theory that a shared `WasiCtx` and both p2/p3 linkers made it target-independent. **Empirically wrong** — both preopen E2E cases trap at `filesystem_method_descriptor_open_at`; the p3 linker attaches but the preopened-directory capability is not wired into the p3 filesystem interface. Reverted, both cases green, finding recorded in the source comments. Merging does not require a uniform target
- **`plugin-bench-test` stays separate** (749 lines / 29 commands): a benchmark fixture is a different kind of artefact from a functional closed-loop fixture
- **A latent fixture-staleness bug found and fixed**: the consolidated freshness check watched only the merged crate's own three files, and it short-circuits *before* invoking cargo — so "cargo's dependency fingerprint will notice the SDK changed" does not hold. A change to the SDK left every cached fixture artifact stale: **tests stayed green while exercising the old artifact**. It now walks the SDK's source trees (recursively, rather than an enumerated file list — the old per-builder lists were themselves the same bug waiting to happen) and rebuilds when anything under them is newer. Verified against a cold cache: all six features rebuilt from scratch, suite green
- **Per-fixture manifests**: the merged crate keeps one `<fixture>.json` per fixture; the root `plugin.json` belongs to the `sdk` fixture because `#[plugin_api]` reads that exact path at compile time (ADR 0005 single source of truth) and cross-checks the trait's method names against its `api` field — pointing the module at a *second* copy would have made that anti-drift comparison vacuous
- **One unexplained transient observed, not reproduced**: the first full run after the four wasm application artifacts were rebuilt reported 1055 passed / 2 failed; five subsequent runs (including one with all fixture artifacts deleted, forcing six live rebuilds) were all 1057 green. Most likely an overlap with the application-artifact rebuild, but that is a hypothesis — the failing test names were not captured and it did not recur
- **Verified**: host `cargo test` **lib 1057 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored**, all integration targets green (broadcast_shutdown, build_manifest_smoke, error_envelope_integration, http_auth_biometric, link_crypto_http, pty_session_chain, server_integration, ws_auth_rules); `scripts/wasip3-toolchain.sh fixture` still produces a magic-validated Component off the merged crate. `bedcode.wit` unchanged, so no ABI implications

#### Terminal history truncation no longer toasts — silent on both ends (desktop wasm app `com.bedcode.terminal-session` + mobile; **no WIT/ABI move**)

- **What**: when history exceeds the cache (ring eviction / truncation), neither the desktop nor the mobile end prompts the user anymore — one background log line remains for diagnostics. Aligned with mature terminal products (no prompt; the user is none the wiser). Also removed the orphaned `desktop.terminal.historyTruncated` host copy key and the plugin/mobile i18n keys that had no consumers left

#### SDK `wasm_entry!` stops logging guest-reported failures at error level (desktop SDK behaviour; all four wasm application artifacts rebuilt; SDK package needs re-publishing)

- **What**: all ten failure paths inside the `wasm_entry!` macro — `activate` / `deactivate` / `on_startup` / `on_shutdown` / `on_message` / `on_message_binary` / `on_process_done` / `on_ws_message` / `on_ws_client_message` / `on_task_event` — now call `log_warn` instead of `log_error`
- **Why**: AGENTS.md §8 states a hard rule — *「guest 自报的可处理错误不升 `error!`」*. These `Err` values are the plugin reporting its own failure (the inner layer of the two-layer `Result`), not host-side evidence. Genuine host faults — traps, store poisoning, fuel exhaustion — are already logged `error!` and counted by the host runtime itself, so downgrading these does not lose a single host failure; it stops `error`-level from degrading into "some plugin returned an `Err`", which is what made the level unusable as an alert signal
- **How it stayed wrong for so long**: the contract lock (`engine_limits::test_component_guest_self_reported_failure_no_host_error`) only ever ran against the hand-written wit-bindgen fixture, which returns `Err(String)` directly and never goes through this macro. The SDK path had never been put in front of that assertion. Consolidating the fixtures onto the SDK binding is what finally exposed it
- **Blast radius**: SDK behaviour change, so all four wasm application artifacts were rebuilt (`pnpm run build`, wasmHash re-injected) and the SDK package needs a re-publish. No WIT change, no ABI number move, no host code change
- **Operational note**: anyone alerting on `error`-level plugin logs will stop seeing guest-reported failures there. They remain in the log at `warn` with the same message, and host-side faults are unaffected
- **Verified**: SDK crate 158 green + `wasm32-wasip3` release build clean; host `cargo test` lib 1057 green, all integration targets green

#### Biometric credential handling moves entirely into the auth centre — public-key custody and signature verification leave the host (B-downsink, desktop ABI v33 → v34; **breaking: devices with a bound biometric credential must re-bind**; mobile WIT/ABI unchanged)

- **Why**: the biometric **orchestration** (challenge issue / single-use / expiry, pairing checks, HTTP endpoints) already lived in the auth centre (`terminal-session` plugin); only two host pieces remained — public-key custody (`biometric:<fp>` rows in `plugin_secrets`) and P-256 signature verification.
